使用常规超声程序代码识别正在进行的妊娠,以便及时吸收孕产妇 疫苗 的 监测:一项挪威注册研究。

Identification of ongoing pregnancies using routine ultrasound procedure codes for timely surveillance of maternal vaccine uptake: A Norwegian registry study.
作者： Melanie Stecher, Margrethe Greve-Isdahl, Jesper Dahl, Evy Dvergsdal, Suzanne Campbell, Fredrik Skår, Inger Johanne Bakken, Hilde M. Engjom, Hinta Meijerink
摘要中文翻译目的:及时监测孕产妇 疫苗接种 对于指导免疫策略至关重要,但卫生登记处通常无法识别正在进行的妊娠,导致 监测 延迟。我们使用 2022 年引入的常规收集的妊娠特异性超声程序代码来评估基于注册的方法,以识别正在进行的妊娠,并以流感 疫苗接种 为例证明及时 疫苗接种 监测 的可行性。方法:我们将来自挪威医学出生登记处 (MBRN)、挪威患者登记处 (NPR) 和免疫登记处的基于人群的登记数据联系起来。NPR 中的持续妊娠是使用妊娠中期超声程序代码(场景 1)或妊娠早期和中期代码的组合并针对流产和缺勤进行调整(场景 2)来识别的。将 NPR 中确定的持续妊娠作为分母的 疫苗接种 吸收率估计值与使用 MBRN 数据中的出生数据作为分母的估计值(黄金标准)进行比较。从 2022 年 10 月至 2023 年 2 月评估了 季节性流感 季节性流感 季节性流感 的月度和累计摄入量。结果:使用方案 1 和 2 分别识别出总共 26,996 名和 27,940 名孕妇,而使用 MBRN 数据则识别出 27,513 名孕妇。每月 疫苗接种 吸收量估计与黄金标准的偏差不到 1.5%。场景 1 和 2 的 疫苗接种 累积使用率为 27.1% (7328/26,996) 和 26.9% (7503/27,940),而使用 MBRN 数据时为 27.8% (7656/27,513)。结论:可以通过管理医疗数据中的超声程序代码来识别正在进行的怀孕,从而在怀孕期间实现强大而及时的 疫苗接种 监测。该方法支持近乎实时的 疫苗接种 监测,并且可能适用于更广泛的产前护理 监测。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
及时监测孕产妇 疫苗接种 对于指导免疫策略至关重要,但卫生登记处通常无法识别持续妊娠,导致 监测 延迟。
设计与对象
我们使用 2022 年引入的常规收集的妊娠特异性超声程序代码来评估基于注册的方法,以识别正在进行的妊娠,并以流感 疫苗接种 为例证明及时 疫苗接种 监测 的可行性。
核心方法
我们链接了来自挪威医学出生登记处、挪威患者登记处和免疫登记处的基于人群的登记数据。挪威患者登记册中的持续妊娠是使用妊娠中期超声程序代码(场景 1)或妊娠早期和中期代码的组合并针对流产和缺勤进行调整(场景 2)来识别的。
主要结果
使用场景 1 和场景 2 分别识别出 26,996 名和 27,940 名孕妇,而使用医学出生登记数据则识别出 27,513 名孕妇。每月 疫苗接种 吸收量估计与黄金标准的偏差不到 1.5%。场景 1 和 2 的 疫苗接种 累计使用率为 27.1% (7328/26,996) 和 26.9% (7503/27,940),而使用医学出生登记数据时为 27.8% (7656/27,513)。
解释与创新
可以通过管理医疗数据中的超声程序代码来识别正在进行的怀孕,从而在怀孕期间实现强大而及时的 疫苗接种 监测。该方法支持近乎实时的 疫苗接种 监测,并且可能适用于更广泛的产前护理 监测。
科研与公卫意义
该方法可以近乎实时地监测孕产妇 疫苗接种 的吸收情况,从而可以在流感季节为及时的公共卫生干预和政策调整提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于行政医疗保健数据和特定的超声程序代码,这些代码可能无法捕获所有怀孕情况或解释报告中的所有偏差。由于潜在的漏报和检查的流感季节范围有限,证据强度中等。

Identification of ongoing pregnancies using routine ultrasound procedure codes for timely surveillance of maternal vaccine uptake: A Norwegian registry study.

Authors: Melanie Stecher, Margrethe Greve-Isdahl, Jesper Dahl, Evy Dvergsdal, Suzanne Campbell, Fredrik Skår, Inger Johanne Bakken, Hilde M. Engjom, Hinta Meijerink
Original abstract (English)
AIM: Timely monitoring of maternal vaccination is essential for guiding immunization strategies, but ongoing pregnancies are usually not identified in health registries, resulting in delayed surveillance. We evaluated a registry-based method using routinely collected pregnancy-specific ultrasound procedure codes introduced in 2022 to identify ongoing pregnancies and demonstrate feasibility of timely vaccination surveillance, using influenza vaccination as an example. METHODS: We linked population-based registry data from the Medical Birth Registry of Norway (MBRN), the Norwegian Patient Register (NPR), and the Immunisation Registry. Ongoing pregnancies in the NPR were identified using either second-trimester ultrasound procedure codes (Scenario 1) or a combination of first- and second-trimester codes with adjustments for pregnancy loss and non-attendance (Scenario 2). Estimates of vaccination uptake using ongoing pregnancies identified in the NPR as denominator were compared with estimates using birth data from the MBRN data as denominator (gold standard). Monthly and cumulative seasonal influenza vaccination uptake was assessed from October 2022 to February 2023. RESULTS: A total of 26,996 and 27,940 pregnant women were identified using Scenarios 1 and 2, respectively, compared with 27,513 using MBRN data. Monthly vaccination uptake estimates deviated by less than 1.5% from the gold standard. Cumulative vaccination uptake was 27.1% (7328/26,996) and 26.9% (7503/27,940) for Scenarios 1 and 2, compared with 27.8% (7656/27,513) using MBRN data. CONCLUSIONS: Ongoing pregnancies can be identified through ultrasound procedure codes from administrative healthcare data, enabling robust and timely vaccination surveillance during pregnancy. The approach supports near real-time vaccination monitoring and might be applicable to broader antenatal care surveillance in general.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Timely monitoring of maternal vaccination is essential for guiding immunization strategies, but ongoing pregnancies are usually not identified in health registries, resulting in delayed surveillance.
Design and population
We evaluated a registry-based method using routinely collected pregnancy-specific ultrasound procedure codes introduced in 2022 to identify ongoing pregnancies and demonstrate feasibility of timely vaccination surveillance, using influenza vaccination as an example.
Core methods
We linked population-based registry data from the Medical Birth Registry of Norway, the Norwegian Patient Register, and the Immunisation Registry. Ongoing pregnancies in the Norwegian Patient Register were identified using either second-trimester ultrasound procedure codes (Scenario 1) or a combination of first- and second-trimester codes with adjustments for pregnancy loss and non-attendance (Scenario 2).
Main results
A total of 26,996 and 27,940 pregnant women were identified using Scenarios 1 and 2, respectively, compared with 27,513 using Medical Birth Registry data. Monthly vaccination uptake estimates deviated by less than 1.5% from the gold standard. Cumulative vaccination uptake was 27.1% (7328/26,996) and 26.9% (7503/27,940) for Scenarios 1 and 2, compared with 27.8% (7656/27,513) using Medical Birth Registry data.
Interpretation and novelty
Ongoing pregnancies can be identified through ultrasound procedure codes from administrative healthcare data, enabling robust and timely vaccination surveillance during pregnancy. The approach supports near real-time vaccination monitoring and might be applicable to broader antenatal care surveillance in general.
Scientific and public-health significance
This method enables near real-time monitoring of maternal vaccination uptake, which can inform timely public health interventions and policy adjustments during influenza seasons.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on administrative healthcare data and specific ultrasound procedure codes, which may not capture all pregnancies or account for all biases in reporting. The evidence strength is moderate due to potential underreporting and the limited scope of the influenza season examined.

猪中流行的甲型流感病毒的职业暴露和一般人群抗体谱作为人畜共患病风险的指示。

Occupationally Exposed and General Population Antibody Profiles to Influenza A Viruses Circulating in Swine as Indication of Zoonotic Risk.
作者： Celeste A Snyder, Garrett M. Janzen, Giovana Ciacci Zanella, Daniel C. A. Moraes, Gustavo S Silva, Jefferson J. S. Santos, Elizabeth M Drapeau, Scott E. Hensley, Tavis K Anderson, Phillip C. Gauger
摘要中文翻译与猪有职业接触的人员可能面临人畜共患病甲型猪流感病毒的高风险。为了评估人类抗体反应,我们通过针对代表性猪和人 季节性流感 疫苗 品系的血凝抑制测定,测试了来自猪兽医、农场雇员和一般人群 队列研究 的血清或血浆。我们通过抗原制图分析了血凝抑制数据,以评估菌株关系和繁殖数模型,以使用年龄分层免疫谱来评估大流行的可能性。与一般人群 队列研究 相比,职业暴露群体的人类季节性 疫苗 摄取量较低(45.5% 比 70%),且猪的几种 H1 和 H3 菌株血清阳性的几率也较低。一种猪品系表现出与其最接近的疫苗品系显着的抗原漂移(3.62抗原单位)。多个菌株所需的大流行传播繁殖数阈值(1.09-1.35)低于记录的大流行菌株(1.46-1.80),这表明群体免疫差距增加了流行的猪H1和H3菌株的人畜共患风险。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
与猪有职业接触的人员可能面临人畜共患病甲型猪流感病毒的高风险。
设计与对象
为了评估人类抗体反应,我们通过针对代表性猪和人 季节性流感 疫苗 品系的血凝抑制测定,测试了来自猪兽医、农场雇员和一般人群 队列研究 的血清或血浆。
核心方法
我们通过抗原制图分析了血凝抑制数据,以评估菌株关系和繁殖数模型,以使用年龄分层免疫谱来评估大流行的可能性。
主要结果
与一般人群 队列研究 相比,职业暴露群体的人类季节性 疫苗 摄取量较低(45.5% 比 70%),且猪的几种 H1 和 H3 菌株血清阳性的几率也较低。一种猪品系表现出与其最接近的疫苗品系显着的抗原漂移(3.62抗原单位)。
解释与创新
多个菌株所需的大流行传播繁殖数阈值(1.09-1.35)低于记录的大流行菌株(1.46-1.80),这表明群体免疫差距增加了流行的猪H1和H3菌株的人畜共患风险。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,职业接触猪可能会增加人畜共患风险,抗原漂移 和较低的繁殖数阈值凸显了加强 监测 和 疫苗接种 策略的必要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于血凝抑制测定和建模,这可能无法捕获 免疫应答 或大流行潜力的所有方面。由于观察设计和对血清学数据的依赖,证据强度中等。

Occupationally Exposed and General Population Antibody Profiles to Influenza A Viruses Circulating in Swine as Indication of Zoonotic Risk.

Authors: Celeste A Snyder, Garrett M. Janzen, Giovana Ciacci Zanella, Daniel C. A. Moraes, Gustavo S Silva, Jefferson J. S. Santos, Elizabeth M Drapeau, Scott E. Hensley, Tavis K Anderson, Phillip C. Gauger
Original abstract (English)
Persons with occupational exposure to swine might be at disproportionate risk for zoonotic swine influenza A virus. To evaluate human antibody responses, we tested serum or plasma from swine veterinarian, farm employee, and general population cohorts by hemagglutination inhibition assays against representative swine and human seasonal influenza vaccine strains. We analyzed hemagglutination inhibition data by antigenic cartography to assess strain relationships and reproduction number modeling to evaluate pandemic potential using age-stratified immunity profiles. Occupationally exposed groups had lower human seasonal vaccine uptake (45.5% vs. 70%) and lower odds of seropositivity to several H1 and H3 strains from swine than did general population cohorts. One swine strain exhibited significant antigenic drift (3.62 antigenic units) from its nearest vaccine strain. Multiple strains required lower reproduction number thresholds for pandemic spread (1.09-1.35) than recorded pandemic strains (1.46-1.80), demonstrating that population immunity gaps heighten zoonotic risk to circulating swine H1 and H3 strains.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Persons with occupational exposure to swine might be at disproportionate risk for zoonotic swine influenza A virus.
Design and population
To evaluate human antibody responses, we tested serum or plasma from swine veterinarian, farm employee, and general population cohorts by hemagglutination inhibition assays against representative swine and human seasonal influenza vaccine strains.
Core methods
We analyzed hemagglutination inhibition data by antigenic cartography to assess strain relationships and reproduction number modeling to evaluate pandemic potential using age-stratified immunity profiles.
Main results
Occupationally exposed groups had lower human seasonal vaccine uptake (45.5% vs. 70%) and lower odds of seropositivity to several H1 and H3 strains from swine than did general population cohorts. One swine strain exhibited significant antigenic drift (3.62 antigenic units) from its nearest vaccine strain.
Interpretation and novelty
Multiple strains required lower reproduction number thresholds for pandemic spread (1.09-1.35) than recorded pandemic strains (1.46-1.80), demonstrating that population immunity gaps heighten zoonotic risk to circulating swine H1 and H3 strains.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings indicate that occupational exposure to swine may increase zoonotic risk, and the antigenic drift and lower reproduction number thresholds highlight the need for enhanced surveillance and vaccination strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relied on hemagglutination inhibition assays and modeling, which may not capture all aspects of immune response or pandemic potential. The evidence strength is moderate due to the observational design and reliance on serological data.

中国大陆首次报告人感染猪源甲型H1N2流感病毒:通过流感样疾病监测确诊病例,昆明(云南),2025年

First report of human infection caused by swine-origin influenza A(H1N2)v virus in mainland China: a confirmed case identified through influenza-like illness surveillance, Kunming (Yunnan), 2025
作者： Dan Zhao, Siyi Luo, Xiaodie Zhang, Zhipeng Mao, Yang Zhou, Min Dai, Ming Zeng, Lu Chao, Hongmei Pan
摘要中文翻译目的 详细介绍中国大陆首例人源甲型H1N2流感病毒感染病例的流行病学特征,为制定人源性甲型H1N2流感病毒感染防控指南提供依据。方法通过现场流行病学调查结合实验室检测,收集该病例的临床和流行病学资料。从患者、密切接触者、动物受试者(包括一头死猪)和环境基质中收集样本并进行分析,以追踪潜在的感染源并描述临床和流行病学特征。结果本研究描述了中国大陆发生的一例散发性、孤立的人感染甲型H1N2猪流感病例,没有人传人传播的证据。症状出现前,患者居住在高风险混合住宅环境中,经常近距离接触家畜和家禽。特别是,他的联排别墅毗邻他叔公的住所,那里养猪;叔公还记录了一只猪的死亡率。但没有接触过出现类似临床表现的人类确诊病例。对收集的样本进行检测证实,从死猪身上获得的肺组织样本中呈流感 N2 亚型阳性,而所有剩余的动物和环境样本的检测结果均为阴性。患者最终在临床康复后出院。共有30名密切接触者完成自我健康监测和咽拭子筛查。在整个监测期间,这些人没有出现流感样疾病,所有咽拭子样本检测结果均为阴性。医学监测未发现继发病例,并相应实施了有针对性的环境消毒。结论 初步判断该病例的可能传染源为携带N2亚型甲型流感病毒的死猪。因此,A(H1N2)v等新型流感亚型的增强型监测和改良型猪流感疫苗接种对于有针对性地预防和控制这些新兴病毒引起的人类感染至关重要。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究满足了公共卫生需求,以确定中国大陆首次记录的人类感染猪源甲型H1N2流感病毒的特征,并为此类新出现的人畜共患病提供预防和控制指导。
设计与对象
这是 2025 年在昆明(云南)利用现场 流行病学 和对患者、接触者、动物和环境标本进行实验室检测进行的单一人类病例调查。
核心方法
通过现场调查收集临床和流行病学数据,对患者、密切接触者、动物(包括死猪)和环境的标本进行分析,以追踪感染源并描述特征。
主要结果
调查发现了一例散发性、孤立的人类甲型H1N2病毒感染病例,没有人传人传播的证据,死猪的肺组织中证实了N2亚型阳性,而所有其他动物和环境样本均为阴性。
解释与创新
作者得出结论,携带 N2 亚型甲型流感病毒的死猪可能是感染源,并强调中国大陆首例人类甲型 H1N2 病毒病例的新颖性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果支持增强新型流感亚型(如 A(H1N2)v)的 监测 和改良猪流感 疫苗接种 来预防新出现的猪源病毒对人类的感染。
局限与证据强度
证据仅限于单个案例,没有检测到二次 传播,限制了普遍性,并给更广泛的 传播 动态留下了不确定性。

First report of human infection caused by swine-origin influenza A(H1N2)v virus in mainland China: a confirmed case identified through influenza-like illness surveillance, Kunming (Yunnan), 2025

Authors: Dan Zhao, Siyi Luo, Xiaodie Zhang, Zhipeng Mao, Yang Zhou, Min Dai, Ming Zeng, Lu Chao, Hongmei Pan
Original abstract (English)
Objective This case report details the epidemiological characteristics of the first human case of swine-origin influenza A(H1N2)v infection identified in mainland China, to formulate guidelines for the prevention and control of human swine-origin influenza A (H1N2) virus infections. Methods Clinical and epidemiological data of the case were collected through field epidemiological investigation combined with laboratory testing. Specimens were collected from the patient, close contacts, animals subjects (including one deceased pig), and environmental matrices and analyzed to trace the potential infection source and characterize the clinical and epidemiological features. Results This study describes a sporadic, isolated case of human infection with swine influenza A(H1N2)v in mainland China, with no evidence of human-to-human transmission. Prior to symptom onset, the patient inhabited a high-risk mixed residential setting with frequent close exposure to domestic livestock and poultry. Specially, his townhouse stood adjacent to his great-uncle’s residence, where swine were raised; the great-uncle also documented the mortality of one pig. But had no contact with human confirmed cases presenting similar clinical manifestations. Testing of the collected specimens confirmed influenza N2 subtype positivity in lung tissue specimens obtained from the deceased pigs, whereas all remaining animal and environmental specimens yielded negative test results. The patient was eventually discharged after clinical recovery. A total of 30 close contacts completed self-health monitoring and throat swab screening. No influenza-like illness developed among these individuals throughout the monitoring period, and all throat swab specimens yielded negative test results. No secondary cases were detected during medical monitoring, and targeted environmental disinfection was implemented accordingly. Conclusion The likely source of infection for the reported case was identified as deceased pigs harboring N2-subtype influenza A virus. Therefore, enhanced surveillance of novel influenza subtypes such as A(H1N2)v and improved swine influenza vaccination are crucial for the targeted prevention and control of human infections by these emerging viruses.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the public-health need to characterize the first documented human infection with a swine-origin influenza A(H1N2)v virus in mainland China and to inform prevention and control guidance for such emerging zoonoses.
Design and population
This was a single human case investigation conducted in Kunming (Yunnan) in 2025 using field epidemiology and laboratory testing of patient, contact, animal, and environmental specimens.
Core methods
Clinical and epidemiological data were collected through a field investigation, and specimens from the patient, close contacts, animals (including a deceased pig), and the environment were analyzed to trace the infection source and describe features.
Main results
The investigation identified a sporadic, isolated human case of A(H1N2)v infection with no evidence of human-to-human transmission, and N2-subtype positivity was confirmed in lung tissue from the deceased pig while all other animal and environmental samples were negative.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that deceased pigs harboring N2-subtype influenza A virus were the likely source of infection and emphasized the novelty of the first human A(H1N2)v case in mainland China.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings support enhanced surveillance of novel influenza subtypes such as A(H1N2)v and improved swine influenza vaccination to prevent human infections by emerging swine-origin viruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to a single case with no secondary transmission detected, constraining generalizability and leaving uncertainty about broader transmission dynamics.

SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的关联积极调节流感病毒感染。

SNHG15 Positively Regulates Influenza Virus Infection Through Its Association With miR-153 and RABL2A.
作者： Samuel Pushparaj, Kishore Vaddadi, Chaoqun Huang, Yurong Liang, Dao Xu, Gayan Bamunuarachchi, Prince Jhandai, Keerthana Santhosh, Elizabeth Varghese, Lin Liu
摘要中文翻译流感病毒引起季节性流行,目前的 疫苗 并不能完全预防流感病毒。这项研究重点关注长链非编码 RNA (lncRNA),它们是不编码蛋白质但在各种生物过程中发挥关键作用的 RNA 分子。我们鉴定出 17 个被流感病毒改变的 lncRNA。其中,SNHG15被发现可增强流感病毒感染。使用 CRISPR 干扰和 RNA 干扰敲低 SNHG15 可减少流感病毒感染,而 SNHG15 过度表达可增加病毒复制。我们发现 miR-153-3p 是 SNHG15 的竞争性内源 RNA (ceRNA),可作为抗流感 microRNA,其过度表达会抵消 SNHG15 的前病毒活性。RNA Pull-down 和质谱鉴定出 Rab 样蛋白 2A (RABL2A) 是 SNHG15 的相互作用伙伴。SNHG15 和 RABL2A 均促进流感病毒内化。此外,siRNA 介导的 RABL2A 敲低减少了 SNHG15 介导的病毒内化。总之,SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的相互作用增强甲型流感病毒感染。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
流感病毒引起季节性流行,目前的 疫苗 并不能完全预防流感病毒。这项研究重点关注长链非编码 RNA (lncRNA),它们是不编码蛋白质但在各种生物过程中发挥关键作用的 RNA 分子。
设计与对象
该研究鉴定了 17 个被流感病毒改变的 lncRNA,重点关注 SNHG15 在增强流感病毒感染方面的作用。
核心方法
使用 CRISPR 干扰和 RNA 干扰敲低 SNHG15 可减少流感病毒感染,而 SNHG15 过度表达可增加病毒复制。RNA Pull-down 和质谱鉴定出 Rab 样蛋白 2A (RABL2A) 是 SNHG15 的相互作用伙伴。
主要结果
在改变的lncRNA中,SNHG15被发现可以增强流感病毒感染。SNHG15 的敲低减少了流感病毒的感染,而过度表达则增加了病毒的复制。SNHG15 和 RABL2A 均促进流感病毒内化。
解释与创新
作者解释说,SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的相互作用增强甲型流感病毒感染。新颖之处在于确定了流感病毒感染中涉及lncRNA的特定分子机制。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为针对 SNHG15 或其相互作用伙伴的新型治疗策略的开发提供信息,以抑制流感病毒感染并提高 疫苗 疗效。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于体外实验,可能无法完全代表体内条件。由于研究范围有限且需要在动物模型和临床环境中进一步验证,证据强度中等。

SNHG15 Positively Regulates Influenza Virus Infection Through Its Association With miR-153 and RABL2A.

Authors: Samuel Pushparaj, Kishore Vaddadi, Chaoqun Huang, Yurong Liang, Dao Xu, Gayan Bamunuarachchi, Prince Jhandai, Keerthana Santhosh, Elizabeth Varghese, Lin Liu
Original abstract (English)
Influenza viruses cause seasonal epidemics, and current vaccines do not fully protect against them. This study focuses on long non-coding RNAs (lncRNAs), which are RNA molecules that do not code for proteins but play crucial roles in various biological processes. We identified 17 lncRNAs altered by the influenza virus. Among these, SNHG15 was found to enhance influenza virus infection. Knockdown of SNHG15 using CRISPR interference and RNA interference reduced influenza virus infection, while overexpression of SNHG15 increased virus replication. We found that miR-153-3p, a competitive endogenous RNA (ceRNA) for SNHG15, acts as an anti-influenza microRNA, and its overexpression negated SNHG15's proviral activity. RNA pull-down and mass spectrometry identified Rab-like protein 2A (RABL2A) as an interacting partner of SNHG15. Both SNHG15 and RABL2A facilitated influenza virus internalization. Additionally, siRNA-mediated knockdown of RABL2A reduced SNHG15-mediated virus internalization. In conclusion, SNHG15 enhances influenza A virus infection through its interactions with miR-153 and RABL2A.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Influenza viruses cause seasonal epidemics, and current vaccines do not fully protect against them. This study focuses on long non-coding RNAs (lncRNAs), which are RNA molecules that do not code for proteins but play crucial roles in various biological processes.
Design and population
The study identified 17 lncRNAs altered by the influenza virus, with a focus on SNHG15's role in enhancing influenza virus infection.
Core methods
Knockdown of SNHG15 using CRISPR interference and RNA interference reduced influenza virus infection, while overexpression of SNHG15 increased virus replication. RNA pull-down and mass spectrometry identified Rab-like protein 2A (RABL2A) as an interacting partner of SNHG15.
Main results
Among the altered lncRNAs, SNHG15 was found to enhance influenza virus infection. Knockdown of SNHG15 reduced influenza virus infection, while overexpression increased virus replication. Both SNHG15 and RABL2A facilitated influenza virus internalization.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that SNHG15 enhances influenza A virus infection through its interactions with miR-153 and RABL2A. The novelty lies in identifying this specific molecular mechanism involving lncRNAs in influenza virus infection.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings could inform the development of novel therapeutic strategies targeting SNHG15 or its interacting partners to inhibit influenza virus infection and improve vaccine efficacy.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on in vitro experiments and may not fully represent in vivo conditions. The evidence strength is moderate due to the limited scope of the study and the need for further validation in animal models and clinical settings.

Vaccine Literacy, Knowledge, and Acceptance Among University Students in the West Bank, Palestine: A Comparative Cross-Sectional Study of Health and Non-Health Students

摘要中文翻译摘要背景 疫苗 犹豫仍然是全球重大的公共卫生挑战,疫苗 读写能力越来越被认为是 疫苗 接受度的关键决定因素。关于巴勒斯坦大学生 疫苗 读写能力和接受度的数据很少,而且没有研究直接比较这种情况下健康和非健康学科的这些结果。本研究旨在评估巴勒斯坦大学生的 疫苗 读写能力、知识和接受度,并确定与 疫苗 接受度相关的因素。方法 2025-2026 学年,在巴勒斯坦西岸的多所大学进行了一项横断面研究。共有 633 名大学生完成了通过 Google Forms 分发的结构化、自填式调查问卷。该工具包括四个部分,评估社会人口特征、疫苗接种 历史和信息源、疫苗 知识(10 个二进制项目)以及使用经过验证的五点李克特量表的 疫苗 读写能力和接受度。使用曼-惠特尼 U 检验和卡方检验进行非参数组比较。Spearman 相关和多变量线性回归用于确定 疫苗 接受度的预测因子。结果 参与者的中位年龄为 19 岁(IQR:18-21),其中 72.4% 为女性。所有领域的中位数得分均中等:疫苗 知识 5.0/10(IQR:3.0–6.0)、疫苗 读写能力 3.50/5(IQR:3.0–3.88)和 疫苗 接受度 3.11/5(IQR:2.78–3.56)。虽然 67.8% 的学生表示在过去五年中收到了推荐的 疫苗,但 季节性流感 疫苗接种 的比例极低,仅为 9.5%。与健康相关的学生在知识、读写能力、接受度、疫苗 的吸收和对官方来源的信任方面的得分显着高于非健康学生(所有 p ≤ 0.010)。女学生表现出更高的知识(p = 0.021)和识字分数(p
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估巴勒斯坦大学生的 疫苗 读写能力、知识和接受度,并确定与 疫苗 接受度相关的因素,解决巴勒斯坦大学生 疫苗 读写能力和接受度数据稀缺的问题。
设计与对象
2025-2026 学年,巴勒斯坦西岸多所大学进行了一项横断面研究,涉及 633 名大学生,他们完成了通过 Google Forms 分发的结构化、自填式调查问卷。
核心方法
使用 Mann–Whitney U 检验和卡方检验进行非参数组比较,同时使用 Spearman 相关性和多变量线性回归来确定 疫苗 接受度的预测因素。
主要结果
参与者的中位年龄为 19 岁(IQR:18-21),其中 72.4% 为女性;所有领域的中位数得分均中等:疫苗 知识 5.0/10(IQR:3.0–6.0)、疫苗 读写能力 3.50/5(IQR:3.0–3.88)和 疫苗 接受度 3.11/5(IQR:2.78–3.56);虽然 67.8% 的学生表示在过去五年中收到了推荐的 疫苗,但 季节性流感 疫苗接种 的比例极低,仅为 9.5%。
解释与创新
健康相关学生在知识、读写能力、接受度、疫苗 吸收和对官方来源的信任方面的得分显着高于非健康学生(p ≤ 0.010),并且女学生表现出更高的知识(p = 0.021)和读写能力得分(p < 0.05)。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调需要采取有针对性的干预措施来提高巴勒斯坦大学生的 疫苗 识字率和接受度,特别是 季节性流感 疫苗接种,其水平仍然极低。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到横断面设计的限制,无法建立因果关系,并且依赖自我报告的数据,可能会引入回忆偏差;然而,大样本量和经过验证的仪器提供了中等的证据强度。

Vaccine Literacy, Knowledge, and Acceptance Among University Students in the West Bank, Palestine: A Comparative Cross-Sectional Study of Health and Non-Health Students

Authors: Saif Al-din Asfour, Bara Sawalmeh, Salma Jabr, Fahmee Mahareeq, Dawod Jawabreh, Hasan Khouli
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Vaccine hesitancy remains a significant public health challenge globally, and vaccine literacy is increasingly recognized as a key determinant of vaccine acceptance. Data on vaccine literacy and acceptance among university students in Palestine are scarce, and no study has directly compared these outcomes across health and non-health disciplines in this setting. This study aimed to assess vaccine literacy, knowledge, and acceptance among Palestinian university students and identify associated factors with vaccine acceptance. Methods A cross-sectional study was conducted at multiple universities across the West Bank, Palestine, during the 2025–2026 academic year. A total of 633 university students completed a structured, self-administered questionnaire distributed via Google Forms. The instrument comprised four sections assessing sociodemographic characteristics, vaccination history and information sources, vaccine knowledge (10 binary items), and vaccine literacy and acceptance using validated five-point Likert scales. Non-parametric group comparisons were performed using the Mann–Whitney U test and Chi-square test. Spearman correlation and multivariable linear regression were used to identify predictors of vaccine acceptance. Results The median age of participants was 19 years (IQR: 18–21), and 72.4% were female. Median scores were moderate across all domains: vaccine knowledge 5.0/10 (IQR: 3.0–6.0), vaccine literacy 3.50/5 (IQR: 3.0–3.88), and vaccine acceptance 3.11/5 (IQR: 2.78–3.56). While 67.8% of students reported receiving recommended vaccines in the past five years, seasonal influenza vaccination was critically low at 9.5%. Health-related students scored significantly higher than non-health students in knowledge, literacy, acceptance, vaccine uptake, and trust in official sources (all p ≤ 0.010). View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to assess vaccine literacy, knowledge, and acceptance among Palestinian university students and identify associated factors with vaccine acceptance, addressing the scarcity of data on vaccine literacy and acceptance among university students in Palestine.
Design and population
A cross-sectional study was conducted at multiple universities across the West Bank, Palestine, during the 2025–2026 academic year, involving 633 university students who completed a structured, self-administered questionnaire distributed via Google Forms.
Core methods
Non-parametric group comparisons were performed using the Mann–Whitney U test and Chi-square test, while Spearman correlation and multivariable linear regression were used to identify predictors of vaccine acceptance.
Main results
The median age of participants was 19 years (IQR: 18–21), and 72.4% were female; median scores were moderate across all domains: vaccine knowledge 5.0/10 (IQR: 3.0–6.0), vaccine literacy 3.50/5 (IQR: 3.0–3.88), and vaccine acceptance 3.11/5 (IQR: 2.78–3.56); while 67.8% of students reported receiving recommended vaccines in the past five years, seasonal influenza vaccination was critically low at 9.5%.
Interpretation and novelty
Health-related students scored significantly higher than non-health students in knowledge, literacy, acceptance, vaccine uptake, and trust in official sources (all p ≤ 0.010), and female students demonstrated higher knowledge (p = 0.021) and literacy scores (p < 0.05).
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the need for targeted interventions to improve vaccine literacy and acceptance among university students in Palestine, particularly for seasonal influenza vaccination, which remains critically low.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was limited by its cross-sectional design, which cannot establish causality, and the reliance on self-reported data, which may introduce recall bias; however, the large sample size and validated instruments provide moderate evidence strength.

COVID-19 疫苗 接受度中的社会影响与恐惧:健康营销传播的见解

Social influence versus fear in COVID-19 vaccine acceptance: insights for health marketing communication
作者： Mario Arias-Oliva, Jorge Pelegrín-Borondo, Jorge de Andrés Sánchez, Mar Souto-Romero, Jorge de Andrés-Sánchez
摘要中文翻译目的 本文研究了恐惧(对 COVID-19 和 COVID-19 疫苗 副作用)和社会影响如何共同和单独影响接受牛津-阿斯利康 疫苗 的意图。本研究旨在通过阐明 疫苗 接受度的情感驱动因素与社会驱动因素的相对重要性,来填补健康和药品营销研究的空白。设计/方法/途径 对居住在西班牙的 600 名成年人进行了定量调查,采用年龄和性别配额抽样(性别配额部分未满足)。受访者使用既定量表评估了对 COVID-19 的恐惧、对 疫苗 副作用的恐惧、社会影响和接种疫苗的意愿。使用一致的偏最小二乘结构方程模型来评估测量有效性并比较三个模型:包括所有预测变量的联合模型、仅恐惧模型和仅社会影响模型。研究结果对 COVID-19 的恐惧对 疫苗接种 意图有积极影响,而对 疫苗 副作用的恐惧则有负面影响。社会影响力显示出强烈的积极影响,并且是所有模型中最具影响力的预测因子。综合考虑时,社会影响力大大降低了恐惧的解释力,这表明受信任的个人和机构比情绪反应更能决定性地制定 疫苗接种 决策。研究限制/影响 结果仅限于特定时间段内的西班牙和牛津-阿斯利康 疫苗。建议在其他国家、疫苗 类型和社会经济环境中进行复制。然而,这些发现可能与类似于 COVID-19 大流行所产生的情况特别相关,在这种情况下,疾病和 疫苗 的潜在影响在很大程度上都是未知的,导致不确定性水平大大高于与既定疾病和 疫苗(例如 季节性流感)相关的不确定性水平。实际影响 健康传播和药品营销策略应优先考虑可信的机构信息和值得信赖的影响者,而不是基于恐惧的诉求。解决错误信息和加强机构信任至关重要。原创性/价值本研究首次对恐惧和社会影响对 COVID-19 疫苗接种 意图的联合与个体影响进行比较分析,提供了原创性的分析重点,为健康营销策略设计提供了新的见解。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本文研究了恐惧(对 COVID-19 和 COVID-19 疫苗 副作用)和社会影响如何共同和单独影响接受牛津-阿斯利康 疫苗 的意图,旨在通过澄清 疫苗 接受的情感与社会驱动因素的相对重要性,填补健康和药品营销研究的空白。
设计与对象
使用年龄和性别配额抽样对居住在西班牙的 600 名成年人进行了定量调查(性别配额部分未得到满足)。
核心方法
受访者使用既定量表评估了对 COVID-19 的恐惧、对 疫苗 副作用的恐惧、社会影响和接种疫苗的意愿,并使用一致的偏最小二乘结构方程模型来评估测量有效性并比较三个模型:包括所有预测因素的联合模型、仅恐惧模型和仅社会影响模型。
主要结果
对 COVID-19 的恐惧对 疫苗接种 意图有积极影响,而对 疫苗 副作用的恐惧则有负面影响。社会影响力显示出强烈的积极影响,并且是所有模型中最具影响力的预测因子。
解释与创新
综合考虑时,社会影响力大大降低了恐惧的解释力,这表明受信任的个人和机构比情绪反应更能决定性地制定 疫苗接种 决策。这项研究通过首次比较分析恐惧的联合效应与个体效应以及社会对 COVID-19 疫苗接种 意图的影响,提供了一个原创的分析重点。
科研与公卫意义
健康传播和药品营销策略应优先考虑可信的机构信息和值得信赖的影响者,而不是基于恐惧的诉求。解决错误信息和加强机构信任至关重要。
局限与证据强度
结果仅限于特定时间段内的西班牙和牛津-阿斯利康 疫苗。建议在其他国家、疫苗 类型和社会经济环境中进行复制。

Social influence versus fear in COVID-19 vaccine acceptance: insights for health marketing communication

Authors: Mario Arias-Oliva, Jorge Pelegrín-Borondo, Jorge de Andrés Sánchez, Mar Souto-Romero, Jorge de Andrés-Sánchez
Original abstract (English)
Purpose This paper examines how fear (of COVID-19 and of COVID-19 vaccine side effects) and social influence jointly and individually affect the intention to receive the Oxford–AstraZeneca vaccine. This study aims to fill a gap in health and pharmaceutical marketing research by clarifying the relative importance of emotional versus social drivers of vaccine acceptance. Design/methodology/approach A quantitative survey of 600 adults residing in Spain was conducted using quota sampling for age and gender (gender quotas were partially unmet). Respondents evaluated fear of COVID-19, fear of vaccine side effects, social influence and intention to vaccinate using established scales. Consistent partial least squares structural equation modelling was used to assess measurement validity and to compare three models: a joint model including all predictors, a fear-only model and a social-influence-only model. Findings Fear of COVID-19 has a positive effect on vaccination intention, while fear of vaccine side effects has a negative effect. Social influence shows a strong positive effect and is the most influential predictor across all models. When considered jointly, social influence substantially reduces the explanatory power of fear, indicating that trusted individuals and institutions shape vaccination decisions more decisively than emotional responses. Research limitations/implications Results are limited to Spain and to the Oxford–AstraZeneca vaccine during a specific time period. Replication in other countries, vaccine types and socio-economic contexts is recommended. Nevertheless, the findings may be particularly relevant for contexts similar to that generated by the COVID-19 pandemic, in which both the disease and the potential effects of the vaccine were largely unknown, resulting in levels of uncertainty substantially higher than those associated with well-established diseases and vaccines, such as seasonal influenza. Practical implications Health communication and pharmaceutical marketing strategies should prioritize credible institutional messaging and trusted influencers rather than fear-based appeals. Addressing misinformation and strengthening institutional trust are essential. Originality/value This study offers an original analytical focus by providing the first comparative analysis of joint versus individual effects of fear and social influence on COVID-19 vaccination intention, offering new insights for health marketing strategy design.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This paper examines how fear (of COVID-19 and of COVID-19 vaccine side effects) and social influence jointly and individually affect the intention to receive the Oxford–AstraZeneca vaccine, aiming to fill a gap in health and pharmaceutical marketing research by clarifying the relative importance of emotional versus social drivers of vaccine acceptance.
Design and population
A quantitative survey of 600 adults residing in Spain was conducted using quota sampling for age and gender (gender quotas were partially unmet).
Core methods
Respondents evaluated fear of COVID-19, fear of vaccine side effects, social influence and intention to vaccinate using established scales, and consistent partial least squares structural equation modelling was used to assess measurement validity and to compare three models: a joint model including all predictors, a fear-only model and a social-influence-only model.
Main results
Fear of COVID-19 has a positive effect on vaccination intention, while fear of vaccine side effects has a negative effect. Social influence shows a strong positive effect and is the most influential predictor across all models.
Interpretation and novelty
When considered jointly, social influence substantially reduces the explanatory power of fear, indicating that trusted individuals and institutions shape vaccination decisions more decisively than emotional responses. This study offers an original analytical focus by providing the first comparative analysis of joint versus individual effects of fear and social influence on COVID-19 vaccination intention.
Scientific and public-health significance
Health communication and pharmaceutical marketing strategies should prioritize credible institutional messaging and trusted influencers rather than fear-based appeals. Addressing misinformation and strengthening institutional trust are essential.
Limitations and evidence strength
Results are limited to Spain and to the Oxford–AstraZeneca vaccine during a specific time period. Replication in other countries, vaccine types and socio-economic contexts is recommended.

FluEvoFormer:用于前瞻性流感抗原 进化 和 疫苗 菌株选择的结构引导生成基础模型

FluEvoFormer: A Structure-Guided Generative Foundation Model for Prospective Influenza Antigenic Evolution and Vaccine Strain Selection
作者： Pankaj Agarwal, Sumendra Yogarayan, Md. Shohel Sayeed
摘要中文翻译季节性流感 疫苗 菌株选择仍然具有挑战性,因为在提出 疫苗 建议后,循环病毒可能会发生漂移。本研究提出了 FluEvoFormer,这是一种结构引导的生成基础模型,用于前瞻性流感抗原 进化 预测和 疫苗 菌株排名。该框架联合编码血凝素和神经氨酸酶序列并合并残基接触图。单独的预测头估计未来的病毒优势和 疫苗 病毒抗原匹配。然后使用受控的类似未来的变体压力测试、不确定性调整和进化枝平衡来对 疫苗 候选者进行排名。对涉及流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 病毒和流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒的目标季节进行了滚动回顾性评估。评估使用截止限制序列记录、血凝抑制数据、疫苗-组成记录、蛋白质结构资源和疫苗-有效性指标。甲型H1N1流感病毒的历史训练语料库还包含2009年之前的季节性记录。FluEvoFormer 实现了最低的保留抗原性预测误差,合并历史甲型 H1N1 流感病毒语料库的平均绝对误差 (MAE) 值为 0.389,流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒语料库的平均绝对误差 (MAE) 值为 0.456。它还改进了未来的优势预测,Kullback-Leibler (KL) 散度值分别为 0.255 和 0.289。该模型在 10 个流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 病毒季节中的 7 个和 10 个流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒季节中的 9 个中选择了具有较高经验归一化覆盖分数的候选者。预测的覆盖分数与外部 疫苗 有效性估计呈很强的正相关。这些发现支持 FluEvoFormer 作为计算决策支持框架,用于在下游实验室和公共卫生评估之前优先考虑流感 疫苗 候选者。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
季节性流感 疫苗 菌株选择仍然具有挑战性,因为在提出 疫苗 建议后,循环病毒可能会发生漂移。
设计与对象
本研究提出了 FluEvoFormer,这是一种结构引导的生成基础模型,用于前瞻性流感抗原 进化 预测和 疫苗 菌株排名。对涉及流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 病毒和流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒的目标季节进行了滚动回顾性评估。
核心方法
该框架联合编码血凝素和神经氨酸酶序列并合并残基接触图。单独的预测头估计未来的病毒优势和 疫苗 病毒抗原匹配。然后使用受控的类似未来的变体压力测试、不确定性调整和进化枝平衡来对 疫苗 候选者进行排名。
主要结果
FluEvoFormer 实现了最低的保留抗原性预测误差,合并历史甲型 H1N1 流感病毒语料库的平均绝对误差 (MAE) 值为 0.389,流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒语料库的平均绝对误差 (MAE) 值为 0.456。它还改进了未来的优势预测,Kullback-Leibler (KL) 散度值分别为 0.255 和 0.289。该模型在 10 个流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 病毒季节中的 7 个和 10 个流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒季节中的 9 个中选择了具有较高经验归一化覆盖分数的候选者。
解释与创新
预测的覆盖分数与外部 疫苗 有效性估计呈很强的正相关。这些发现支持 FluEvoFormer 作为计算决策支持框架,用于在下游实验室和公共卫生评估之前优先考虑流感 疫苗 候选者。
科研与公卫意义
FluEvoFormer 提供了一种计算工具,通过预测抗原 进化 和对候选菌株进行排名来改进流感 疫苗 菌株的选择,从而有可能增强 疫苗 的有效性和公共卫生反应。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于回顾性评估和历史数据,可能无法完全捕捉未来的病毒 进化 动态。由于仅具有抽象性质并且依赖计算模型而没有现实世界的验证,因此证据强度中等。

FluEvoFormer: A Structure-Guided Generative Foundation Model for Prospective Influenza Antigenic Evolution and Vaccine Strain Selection

Authors: Pankaj Agarwal, Sumendra Yogarayan, Md. Shohel Sayeed
Original abstract (English)
Seasonal influenza vaccine strain selection remains challenging because circulating viruses may drift after vaccine recommendations are made. This study presents FluEvoFormer, a structure-guided generative foundation model for prospective influenza antigenic evolution forecasting and vaccine strain ranking. The framework jointly encodes hemagglutinin and neuraminidase sequences and incorporates residue-contact graphs. Separate prediction heads estimate future viral dominance and the vaccine–virus antigenic match. Controlled future-like variant stress testing, uncertainty adjustments, and clade balancing are then used to rank vaccine candidates. A rolling retrospective evaluation was performed for target seasons involving the influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 virus and influenza A(H3N2) virus. The evaluation used cutoff-restricted sequence records, hemagglutination inhibition data, vaccine-composition records, protein-structure resources, and vaccine-effectiveness indicators. The historical training corpus for the influenza A(H1N1) virus also contained pre-2009 seasonal records. FluEvoFormer achieved the lowest held-out antigenicity prediction error, with mean absolute error (MAE) values of 0.389 for the combined historical influenza A(H1N1) virus corpus and 0.456 for the influenza A(H3N2) virus corpus. It also improved the future dominance prediction, with Kullback–Leibler (KL) divergence values of 0.255 and 0.289, respectively. The model selected candidates with higher empirical normalized coverage scores in seven out of 10 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 virus seasons and nine out of 10 influenza A(H3N2) virus seasons. The predicted coverage score showed a strong positive correlation with external vaccine-effectiveness estimates. These findings support FluEvoFormer as a computational decision-support framework for prioritizing influenza vaccine candidates before downstream laboratory and public health evaluations.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Seasonal influenza vaccine strain selection remains challenging because circulating viruses may drift after vaccine recommendations are made.
Design and population
This study presents FluEvoFormer, a structure-guided generative foundation model for prospective influenza antigenic evolution forecasting and vaccine strain ranking. A rolling retrospective evaluation was performed for target seasons involving the influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 virus and influenza A(H3N2) virus.
Core methods
The framework jointly encodes hemagglutinin and neuraminidase sequences and incorporates residue-contact graphs. Separate prediction heads estimate future viral dominance and the vaccine–virus antigenic match. Controlled future-like variant stress testing, uncertainty adjustments, and clade balancing are then used to rank vaccine candidates.
Main results
FluEvoFormer achieved the lowest held-out antigenicity prediction error, with mean absolute error (MAE) values of 0.389 for the combined historical influenza A(H1N1) virus corpus and 0.456 for the influenza A(H3N2) virus corpus. It also improved the future dominance prediction, with Kullback–Leibler (KL) divergence values of 0.255 and 0.289, respectively. The model selected candidates with higher empirical normalized coverage scores in seven out of 10 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 virus seasons and nine out of 10 influenza A(H3N2) virus seasons.
Interpretation and novelty
The predicted coverage score showed a strong positive correlation with external vaccine-effectiveness estimates. These findings support FluEvoFormer as a computational decision-support framework for prioritizing influenza vaccine candidates before downstream laboratory and public health evaluations.
Scientific and public-health significance
FluEvoFormer provides a computational tool to improve influenza vaccine strain selection by forecasting antigenic evolution and ranking candidates, potentially enhancing vaccine effectiveness and public health responses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on retrospective evaluation and historical data, which may not fully capture future viral evolution dynamics. The evidence strength is moderate due to the abstract-only nature and reliance on computational modeling without real-world validation.

使用时间序列基础模型进行多元流感预测:利用香港 11 年 监测 数据与经典统计模型的比较研究

Multivariate influenza forecasting using time-series foundation models: a comparative study with classical statistical models using 11 years of surveillance data in Hong Kong
作者： Rachel Yui Ki Chu, Kam Chiu Szeto, Pui Hong Chung
摘要中文翻译准确的短期流感活动预测对于公共卫生准备工作至关重要,但对于依赖长期历史数据且经常因数据稀缺而失败的经典统计模型仍然具有挑战性。在大型异构数据集上进行预训练的时间序列基础模型(TSFM)可以通过可转移的时间表示来提供更高的准确性和数据效率。我们分析了香港(2014-2025年)605周的流感监测数据,包括就诊率、实验室检测、住院情况、重症病例和学校暴发。将六种 TSFM(Google TimesFM、Amazon Chronos、IBM Granite Tiny Time Mixers [TTM]、Timer-XL、Moirai-MoE 和 UniTS)与滚动原点设计中的 OLS、GLM、SARIMA 和经验贝叶斯模型进行比较,生成一到四周的预测。使用 R 2 、RMSE、对称 MAPE、加权区间评分 (WIS) 和多变量残差诊断来评估预测准确性。我们进一步估计了 R 2 ≥ 0.50 所需的最小输入窗口和最大可信预测范围(R 2 ≥ 0.50,具有显着的 RMSE 增益,与 Chronos、TimesFM 和 SARIMA 相比,实现了最佳整体一周性能(R 2 0.88–0.89;RMSE 30.6–31.3),显着优于 OLS(R 2 0.78;p < 0.0001)和 GLM(R 2 0.79;p < 0.0001)在两到四周的表现中,Chronos 能够对长达三周的多变量流感 监测 进行可靠的预测(R 2 ≥ 0.50;p < 0.05),而经典模型则在两周后低于所需的阈值。与经典模型相比,至少需要 297 周的输入数据才能实现 R 2 ≥ 0.50,TSFM 可以提供相对更准确、校准良好且数据效率更高的流行病预测,与经典回归模型相比,它们能够从最少的历史数据中生成更可靠的预测,这支持了它们在 季节性流感 监测 和新出现的流行病(例如 COVID-19)中快速部署的潜力。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
准确的短期流感活动预测对于公共卫生准备工作至关重要,但对于依赖长期历史数据且经常因数据稀缺而失败的经典统计模型仍然具有挑战性。
设计与对象
我们分析了香港(2014-2025年)605周的流感监测数据,包括就诊率、实验室检测、住院情况、重症病例和学校暴发。
核心方法
将六种 TSFM(Google TimesFM、Amazon Chronos、IBM Granite Tiny Time Mixers [TTM]、Timer-XL、Moirai-MoE 和 UniTS)与滚动原点设计中的 OLS、GLM、SARIMA 和经验贝叶斯模型进行比较,生成一到四周的预测。使用 R2、RMSE、对称 MAPE、加权区间评分 (WIS) 和多变量残差诊断来评估预测准确性。
主要结果
Chronos、TimesFM 和 SARIMA 取得了最佳整体一周表现(R2 0.88–0.89;RMSE 30.6–31.3),显着优于 OLS(R2 0.78;p < 0.0001)和 GLM(R2 0.79;p < 0.0001)。Chronos 在两到四周的表现中优于其他型号。Chronos 对长达三周的多变量流感 监测 预测进行了可靠的预测(R2 ≥ 0.50;p < 0.05),而经典模型在两周后下降到阈值以下。TSFM 只需 8 周的输入数据即可实现 R2 ≥ 0.50,而经典模型至少需要 297 周。
解释与创新
与经典回归模型相比,TSFM 可提供相对更准确、校准良好且数据高效的流行病预测,捕获时间和变量间的依赖性。与传统方法相比,它们能够从最少的历史数据中生成更可靠的预测,这支持了它们在 季节性流感 监测 和新出现的流行病(例如 COVID-19)中快速部署的潜力。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,TSFM 可以通过用最少的数据提供可靠的流感预测来加强公共卫生准备,从而在季节性 暴发 和大流行期间实现及时的资源分配和干预策略。
局限与证据强度
该研究是使用来自单个地理区域(香港)的 监测 数据进行的,这可能会限制对具有不同流感动态的其他人群的普遍适用性。由于数据集规模大、比较设计严格以及具有统计显着性的结果,证据强度很高。

Multivariate influenza forecasting using time-series foundation models: a comparative study with classical statistical models using 11 years of surveillance data in Hong Kong

Authors: Rachel Yui Ki Chu, Kam Chiu Szeto, Pui Hong Chung
Original abstract (English)
Accurate short-term forecasts of influenza activity are critical for public health preparedness but remain challenging with classical statistical models that depend on long historical data and often fail under data scarcity. Time-series foundation models (TSFMs), pretrained on large heterogeneous datasets, may offer improved accuracy and data efficiency through transferable temporal representations. We analyzed 605 weeks of influenza surveillance data from Hong Kong (2014–2025), including consultation rates, laboratory detections, hospital admissions, severe cases, and school outbreaks. Six TSFMs (Google TimesFM, Amazon Chronos, IBM Granite Tiny Time Mixers [TTM], Timer-XL, Moirai-MoE, and UniTS) were compared with OLS, GLM, SARIMA, and empirical Bayesian models in a rolling-origin design generating one- to four-week forecasts. Forecast accuracy was evaluated using R 2 , RMSE, symmetric MAPE, weighted interval score (WIS) and multivariable residual diagnostics. We further estimated the minimal input window required for R 2 ≥ 0.50 and the maximum credible forecast horizon (R 2 ≥ 0.50 with significant RMSE gain vs. Chronos, TimesFM, and SARIMA achieved the best overall one-week performance (R 2 0.88–0.89; RMSE 30.6–31.3), significantly outperforming OLS (R 2 0.78; p < 0.0001) and GLM (R 2 0.79; p < 0.0001). Chronos outperformed other models in two-to-four-week performance. Chronos produced credible forecasts of the multivariate influenza surveillance predictions up to three weeks (R 2 ≥ 0.50; p < 0.05), whereas classical models fell below threshold beyond two weeks. TSFMs required as low as eight weeks of input data to achieve R 2 ≥ 0.50, compared to at least 297 weeks for classical models. TSFMs deliver comparatively more accurate, well-calibrated, and data-efficient epidemic forecasts, capturing temporal and inter-variable dependencies than classical regression models. Their ability to generate more reliable forecasts from minimal historical data compared to classical approaches supports their potential for rapid deployment in seasonal influenza surveillance and emerging pandemics such as COVID-19.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Accurate short-term forecasts of influenza activity are critical for public health preparedness but remain challenging with classical statistical models that depend on long historical data and often fail under data scarcity.
Design and population
We analyzed 605 weeks of influenza surveillance data from Hong Kong (2014–2025), including consultation rates, laboratory detections, hospital admissions, severe cases, and school outbreaks.
Core methods
Six TSFMs (Google TimesFM, Amazon Chronos, IBM Granite Tiny Time Mixers [TTM], Timer-XL, Moirai-MoE, and UniTS) were compared with OLS, GLM, SARIMA, and empirical Bayesian models in a rolling-origin design generating one- to four-week forecasts. Forecast accuracy was evaluated using R2, RMSE, symmetric MAPE, weighted interval score (WIS) and multivariable residual diagnostics.
Main results
Chronos, TimesFM, and SARIMA achieved the best overall one-week performance (R2 0.88–0.89; RMSE 30.6–31.3), significantly outperforming OLS (R2 0.78; p < 0.0001) and GLM (R2 0.79; p < 0.0001). Chronos outperformed other models in two-to-four-week performance. Chronos produced credible forecasts of the multivariate influenza surveillance predictions up to three weeks (R2 ≥ 0.50; p < 0.05), whereas classical models fell below threshold beyond two weeks. TSFMs required as low as eight weeks of input data to achieve R2 ≥ 0.50, compared to at least 297 weeks for classical models.
Interpretation and novelty
TSFMs deliver comparatively more accurate, well-calibrated, and data-efficient epidemic forecasts, capturing temporal and inter-variable dependencies than classical regression models. Their ability to generate more reliable forecasts from minimal historical data compared to classical approaches supports their potential for rapid deployment in seasonal influenza surveillance and emerging pandemics such as COVID-19.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that TSFMs can enhance public health preparedness by providing reliable influenza forecasts with minimal data, enabling timely resource allocation and intervention strategies during seasonal outbreaks and pandemics.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was conducted using surveillance data from a single geographic region (Hong Kong), which may limit generalizability to other populations with different influenza dynamics. The evidence strength is high due to the large dataset size, rigorous comparative design, and statistically significant results.

母体流感 疫苗接种 增强婴儿母乳抗体介导的持久保护

Maternal influenza vaccination boosts durable breast milk antibody-mediated protection in infants
作者： Remziye E. Wessel, Xin Tong, Allison Bhattacharya, Allison R Burrell, Sara Spranger, Ardythe L. Morrow, Daniel C. Payne, Margaret E. Ackerman, Galit Alter, Mary Allen Staat
摘要中文翻译流感给全世界带来了巨大的疾病负担,小婴儿感染严重流感的风险增加。通过胎盘和母乳转移的母体抗体赋予新生儿抗流感免疫力。胎盘移植提供了有限的保护窗口,但母乳抗体移植可以在整个产后期间提供长期的免疫力。然而,母乳抗体谱如何由母体 疫苗接种 形成、在出生后发生变化以及它们是否有助于预防流感仍不完全明确。对纵向出生 队列研究 中 21 种不同抗原的流感特异性抗体的综合系统血清学分析显示,母体流感 疫苗接种 与产后两周母乳中抗体依赖性补体沉积 (ADCD) 和 FcγR 结合增强相关。这种效应通过 疫苗接种 的妊娠期进一步分层:妊娠早期 疫苗 表现出 ADCD 和 IgM 水平增加,而妊娠中期或晚期 疫苗接种 与 FcγR 和 FcαR 结合增加相关。纵向样本分析表明,产后乳汁中的抗流感抗体反应转向增加 FcγR 结合并降低 ADCD 活性和 IgM 水平。最后,母乳中的基线 ADCD 活性和血清 IgG1 水平被确定为生命前六个月对流感感染抵抗力的标志。与单独的任一隔室相比,结合牛奶和血清隔室的保护相关性的多变量模型提供了更大的预测能力,这表明抗体转移的两种途径都增强了婴儿的抗流感免疫力。这些数据共同表明,母体流感 疫苗接种 通过母乳赋予婴儿保护,以增强胎盘抗体转移,并且这些保护性抗体的功能能力可以通过延长母乳喂养而增强。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
流感给全世界带来了巨大的疾病负担,小婴儿感染严重流感的风险增加。通过胎盘和母乳转移的母体抗体赋予新生儿抗流感免疫力。
设计与对象
该研究涉及纵向出生 队列研究,以分析母乳和血清中 21 种不同抗原的流感特异性抗体。
核心方法
进行全面的系统血清学分析,以评估母乳和血清样本中抗体依赖性补体沉积 (ADCD)、FcγR 结合、FcαR 结合和 IgM 水平。
主要结果
母体流感 疫苗接种 与产后两周母乳中 ADCD 和 FcγR 结合增强相关,其影响按 疫苗接种 的三个月期分层。纵向分析显示,产后 FcγR 结合增加,ADCD 活性和 IgM 水平降低。
解释与创新
作者解释说,母体流感 疫苗接种 通过母乳赋予婴儿保护,以增强胎盘抗体转移,并且这些保护性抗体的功能能力可以通过延长母乳喂养而增强。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,可以优化母亲 疫苗接种 和母乳喂养策略,以增强婴儿对流感的保护,母乳中的基线 ADCD 活性和血清 IgG1 水平被确定为抗感染的标志。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于单胎 队列研究 的观察数据,但综合系统血清学方法和纵向设计为母乳抗体的保护作用提供了适度的证据。

Maternal influenza vaccination boosts durable breast milk antibody-mediated protection in infants

Authors: Remziye E. Wessel, Xin Tong, Allison Bhattacharya, Allison R Burrell, Sara Spranger, Ardythe L. Morrow, Daniel C. Payne, Margaret E. Ackerman, Galit Alter, Mary Allen Staat
Original abstract (English)
Influenza contributes to significant disease burden worldwide, and young infants are at an increased risk of severe influenza infection. Maternal antibodies transferred across the placenta and through breast milk confer anti-influenza immunity to the newborn. Placental transfer provides a finite window of protection, but breast milk antibody transfer may provide prolonged immunity across the postnatal period. Yet, how breastmilk antibody profiles are shaped by maternal vaccination, change postnatally, and whether they contribute to protection against influenza remains incompletely defined. Comprehensive systems serological analysis of influenza-specific antibodies across 21 different antigens in a longitudinal birth cohort revealed maternal influenza vaccination was associated with enhanced antibody-dependent complement deposition (ADCD) and FcγR binding in breast milk at two weeks postpartum. This effect was further stratified by trimester of vaccination: first trimester vaccinees exhibited increased ADCD and IgM levels, whereas second or third trimester vaccination was associated with increased FcγR and FcαR binding. Analysis of longitudinal samples revealed that anti-influenza antibody responses in milk shift postpartum toward increased FcγR binding and decreased ADCD activity and IgM levels. Finally, baseline ADCD activity in breast milk and serum IgG1 levels were identified as signatures of resistance to influenza infection during the first six months of life. A multivariate model combining correlates of protection across milk and serum compartments offered greater predictive power compared to either compartment alone, suggesting both routes of antibody transfer bolster anti-influenza immunity in infants. These data collectively suggest that maternal influenza vaccination confers infant protection via breast milk to augment placental antibody transfer, and the functional capacity of these protective antibodies could be enhanced with prolonged breastfeeding.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Influenza contributes to significant disease burden worldwide, and young infants are at an increased risk of severe influenza infection. Maternal antibodies transferred across the placenta and through breast milk confer anti-influenza immunity to the newborn.
Design and population
The study involved a longitudinal birth cohort to analyze influenza-specific antibodies across 21 different antigens in breast milk and serum compartments.
Core methods
Comprehensive systems serological analysis was performed to assess antibody-dependent complement deposition (ADCD), FcγR binding, FcαR binding, and IgM levels in breast milk and serum samples.
Main results
Maternal influenza vaccination was associated with enhanced ADCD and FcγR binding in breast milk at two weeks postpartum, with effects stratified by trimester of vaccination. Longitudinal analysis revealed postpartum shifts toward increased FcγR binding and decreased ADCD activity and IgM levels.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that maternal influenza vaccination confers infant protection via breast milk to augment placental antibody transfer, and the functional capacity of these protective antibodies could be enhanced with prolonged breastfeeding.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings suggest that maternal vaccination and breastfeeding strategies could be optimized to enhance infant protection against influenza, with baseline ADCD activity in breast milk and serum IgG1 levels identified as signatures of resistance to infection.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to observational data from a single birth cohort, but the comprehensive systems serological approach and longitudinal design provide moderate evidence for the protective role of breast milk antibodies.

季前免疫力、年龄、病毒脱落和社区暴露在塑造流感家庭 传播 动态中的作用

The roles of pre-season immunity, age, viral shedding, and community exposures in shaping influenza household transmission dynamics
作者： Molly K. Sauter, Jackie Kleynhans, Jocelyn Moyes, Meredith McMorrow, Florette K. Treurnicht, Orienka Hellferscee, Anne von Gottberg, Nicole Wolter, Amelia Buys, Lorens Maake
摘要中文翻译摘要:我们对流感传播的理解仍然不完善,因为无症状感染的高患病率常常未被检测到。为应对这一挑战,我们利用了来自南非农村和城市地区连续三年的家庭队列研究研究中独特解析的数据。该研究纳入了季前血清采集和流感季节期间每周两次的病毒学检测,无论是否存在症状。我们开发了一种亚型/谱系特异性的流感家庭传播模型,该模型考虑了整个感染临床谱中的时间解析病毒脱落,使我们能够分离家庭和社区暴露、季前免疫力和年龄对传播的影响。我们的分析显示,受感染家庭成员的病毒脱落强度,以循环阈值(Ct)值衡量,与所有四种流感亚型/谱系的传播风险显著相关。在调整病毒脱落后,季前血凝抑制(HAI)滴度大于1:40与甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒和B/Victoria的感染获取风险显著降低相关,但对B/Yamagata则无此关联。值得注意的是,与成人相比,儿童在所有亚型/谱系中表现出更高的易感性和更长的病毒脱落持续时间,即使在调整了季前HAI滴度后也是如此。虽然我们的研究结果支持HAI滴度与保护作用相关,但年龄对易感性和病毒脱落的强烈残余效应可能反映了随时间推移反复暴露所形成的额外免疫应答的积累。我们的研究强调了需要探索超出HAI滴度的免疫机制,这些机制调节流感的易感性和传播。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解决了对流感 传播 动态的不完全理解,特别是经常逃避检测的无症状感染者的作用以及影响家庭传播的因素。
设计与对象
该研究使用了一项在南非农村和城市连续三个流感季节进行的家庭 队列研究 研究,包括重复的季前血清采集和每周两次的病毒学检测,无论症状如何。
核心方法
开发了亚型/谱系特异性流感家庭 传播 模型,将整个临床感染谱中的时间分辨病毒排出数据纳入其中,从而能够分离家庭和社区暴露影响、季前免疫和年龄对 传播 的影响。
主要结果
通过受感染家庭成员的周期阈值测量的病毒脱落强度与所研究的所有四种流感亚型/谱系的 传播 风险增加显着相关。
解释与创新
季前血凝抑制滴度高于 1:40 与 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒 和 B/Victoria 较低的感染风险相关,但与 B/Yamagata 无关,而儿童在所有亚型中都表现出比成人更高的易感性和更长的排毒持续时间,即使在调整季前免疫力后也是如此。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调了病毒脱落强度和年龄特异性易感性在流感 传播 中的重要性,表明血凝抑制滴度之外的免疫机制可能在调节易感性和传播方面发挥关键作用。
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性在于其仅依赖抽象证据,并且缺乏详细的方法或统计报告,这限制了得出的结论的强度。

The roles of pre-season immunity, age, viral shedding, and community exposures in shaping influenza household transmission dynamics

Authors: Molly K. Sauter, Jackie Kleynhans, Jocelyn Moyes, Meredith McMorrow, Florette K. Treurnicht, Orienka Hellferscee, Anne von Gottberg, Nicole Wolter, Amelia Buys, Lorens Maake
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Our understanding of influenza transmission remains imperfect due to the high prevalence of asymptomatic infections that often go undetected. To address this challenge, we leveraged uniquely resolved data from a household cohort study spanning three consecutive years in rural and urban South Africa. The study incorporated pre-season serum collection and twice-weekly virological testing during the influenza season, regardless of symptom presence. We developed a subtype/lineage-specific influenza household transmission model that accounts for time-resolved viral shedding across the full clinical spectrum of infections, allowing us to disentangle the role of household and community exposures, pre-season immunity, and age on transmission. Our analysis revealed that viral shedding intensity, as measured by the cycle threshold (Ct) values of infected household members, significantly correlated with the risk of transmission for all four influenza subtypes/lineages. After adjusting for viral shedding, pre-season hemagglutination inhibition (HAI) titers greater than 1:40 were associated with a significantly lower risk of infection acquisition for A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria, but not for B/Yamagata. Notably, children exhibited higher susceptibility and longer viral shedding durations compared to adults across all subtypes/lineages, even after adjusting for pre-season HAI titers. While our findings support that HAI titers correlate with protection, the strong residual effects of age on susceptibility and viral shedding may reflect the accumulation of additional immune responses shaped by repeated exposures over time. Our study underscores the need to explore immune mechanisms beyond HAI titers that modulate influenza susceptibility and transmission.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses an incomplete understanding of influenza transmission dynamics, particularly the role of asymptomatic infections that often evade detection and the factors shaping household spread.
Design and population
The research used a household cohort study conducted over three consecutive influenza seasons in rural and urban South Africa, involving repeated pre-season serum collection and twice-weekly virological testing regardless of symptoms.
Core methods
A subtype/lineage-specific influenza household transmission model was developed to incorporate time-resolved viral shedding data across the full clinical spectrum of infections, enabling separation of household and community exposure effects, pre-season immunity, and age influences on transmission.
Main results
Viral shedding intensity, measured by cycle threshold values from infected household members, was significantly associated with increased transmission risk for all four influenza subtypes/lineages studied.
Interpretation and novelty
Pre-season hemagglutination inhibition titers above 1:40 were linked to lower infection risk for A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria but not B/Yamagata, while children showed higher susceptibility and longer shedding durations than adults across all subtypes, even after adjusting for pre-season immunity.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the importance of viral shedding intensity and age-specific susceptibility in influenza transmission, suggesting that immune mechanisms beyond hemagglutination inhibition titers may play key roles in modulating susceptibility and spread.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on abstract-only evidence and the absence of detailed methodological or statistical reporting, which constrains the strength of the conclusions drawn.

Visualizing the Effect of pH on Solubilization of the Influenza A Viral Core
作者： TODO TODO
摘要中文翻译资料来源:Stauffer,S. 等人,通过梯度离心对 A 型流感病毒衣壳进行体外拆卸。J. 维斯。过期。(2016)该视频演示了 pH 对甲型流感病毒核心溶解的影响。凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色揭示了酸性条件下核心结构的逐步分解。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在调查 pH 对甲型流感病毒核心溶解的影响,填补理解环境因素如何影响病毒结构的空白。
设计与对象
该研究涉及在受控实验室环境中使用梯度离心对甲型流感病毒衣壳进行体外分解过程。
核心方法
这些方法包括使用凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色来可视化病毒核心在不同酸性条件下的分解。
主要结果
凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色揭示了酸性条件下核心结构的逐步分解,并在不同 pH 水平下观察到特定模式。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为 pH 诱导的病毒核心结构变化的证据,为研究病毒分解提供了一种新颖的可视化技术。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现有助于理解病毒结构动力学,这可能为未来关于病毒稳定性和潜在治疗靶点的研究提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外条件,证据基于目视观察而不是定量分析,导致结论可信度中等。

Visualizing the Effect of pH on Solubilization of the Influenza A Viral Core

Authors: TODO TODO
Original abstract (English)
Source: Stauffer, S. et al., In Vitro Disassembly of Influenza A Virus Capsids by Gradient Centrifugation. J. Vis. Exp. (2016)This video demonstrates the effect of pH on the solubilization of influenza A viral cores. Gel electrophoresis and protein staining reveal the progressive disassembly of the core structure under acidic conditions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the effect of pH on the solubilization of the influenza A viral core, addressing a gap in understanding how environmental factors influence viral structure.
Design and population
The study involved an in vitro disassembly process of influenza A virus capsids using gradient centrifugation, conducted in a controlled laboratory setting.
Core methods
The methods included the use of gel electrophoresis and protein staining to visualize the disassembly of the viral core under varying acidic conditions.
Main results
Gel electrophoresis and protein staining revealed the progressive disassembly of the core structure under acidic conditions, with specific patterns observed at different pH levels.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted these findings as evidence of pH-induced structural changes in the viral core, offering a novel visualization technique for studying viral disassembly.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings contribute to the understanding of viral structural dynamics, which may inform future research on viral stability and potential therapeutic targets.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro conditions, and the evidence is based on visual observation rather than quantitative analysis, resulting in moderate confidence in the conclusions.

原始论文早期流感暴露对婴儿和儿童免疫力的影响:美国流感 IMPRINT 队列研究(预印本)

Original Paper The influence of early influenza exposures on immunity in infants and children: The US Influenza IMPRINT Cohort (Preprint)
作者： Allison Burrell, Nancy Back, Chelsea Rohlfs, Sara Spranger, Shannon C. Conrey, Brendon White, Christina Quigley, Braxton Forde, Jens Wrammert, Jacob E. Kohlmeier
摘要中文翻译背景流感 疫苗 的有效性是可变的,而流感发病率和 疫苗 仍然很高。流感的免疫印记已被描述,并且可能导致 疫苗 性能不佳。不幸的是,缺乏关于妊娠期和儿童期流感自然史的关键数据。适当设计的产妇出生 队列研究 可以识别最早的流感暴露,并且对于解决流感 流行病学 和免疫学方面的差距是必要的,这些差距目前阻碍了我们开发更广泛有效和持久的 疫苗 的能力。目的 我们描述了流感 IMPRINT 队列研究(对先前流感随时间的免疫记忆)的方案,这是一种强化产妇分娩 队列研究,旨在了解早期接触流感抗原如何影响以后生活的反应。方法 IMPRINT 是俄亥俄州辛辛那提市健康母亲和儿童的预产期 队列研究。母亲在妊娠晚期进行登记,并对其孩子进行至少三年的跟踪,登记目标为 1,500 对母子。从出生后第 2 周开始,无论是否存在症状,每周都会收集儿童鼻拭子和每周两次的症状调查。在入学时和入学后的每年夏天从母亲那里收集血清。每年夏天,以及流感感染和 疫苗接种 后,从分娩时的脐带和第 6 周的儿童采集儿童血样。收集全血、血清、血浆和外周血单核细胞。提取医疗记录以获取就医呼吸系统疾病的临床数据。审查国家登记处和医疗记录以获取 疫苗 给药日期。其他收集的样本包括粪便、母乳、唾液和脸颊拭子。流行病学、免疫学和病毒学研究正在进行中,以确定流感暴露如何影响 发病率、亚临床感染、症状学和对后续暴露的免疫反应。结果 筛选和入组开始于 2019 年 10 月,结束于 2025 年 11 月。总共有 1,624 名怀孕母亲临时入组,其中 1,324 名母亲符合研究入组的最终资格标准。参与者的后续行动正在进行中。满足最终资格要求的参与者与未满足最终资格要求的参与者存在显着差异;登记的母亲往往年龄较大,有伴侣,是白人和/或非西班牙裔,有私人保险,家庭收入较高,受教育程度较高。结论 IMPRINT 整合了来自一组互补合作者的先进方法、实验室和流行病学工具,以检测和订购流感感染以及生命第一年经历的 疫苗接种。队列研究 努力在资格磨合期促进母婴对的发展,但也在一项深入的多年研究中成功地跟踪了那些符合资格的母婴对。IMPRINT 提供了一个新的框架,以提高对初次流感暴露的免疫反应的理解,并描述初始暴露和随后的暴露顺序如何影响流感免疫景观。临床试验 ClinicalTrials.gov ID NCT05436184
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
背景流感 疫苗 的有效性是可变的,而流感发病率和 疫苗 仍然很高。流感的免疫印记已被描述,并且可能导致 疫苗 性能不佳。
设计与对象
IMPRINT 是俄亥俄州辛辛那提市健康母亲和儿童的预产期 队列研究。母亲在妊娠晚期进行登记,并对其孩子进行至少三年的跟踪,登记目标为 1,500 对母子。
核心方法
从出生后第 2 周开始,无论是否存在症状,每周都会收集儿童鼻拭子和每周两次的症状调查。在入学时和入学后的每年夏天从母亲那里收集血清。每年夏天,以及流感感染和 疫苗接种 后,从分娩时的脐带和第 6 周的儿童采集儿童血样。
主要结果
筛选和入组开始于 2019 年 10 月,结束于 2025 年 11 月。总共有 1,624 名怀孕母亲临时入组,其中 1,324 名母亲符合研究入组的最终资格标准。参与者的后续行动正在进行中。
解释与创新
IMPRINT 提供了一个新的框架,以提高对初次流感暴露的免疫反应的理解,并描述初始暴露和随后的暴露顺序如何影响流感免疫景观。
科研与公卫意义
IMPRINT 集成了来自一组互补合作者的先进方法、实验室和流行病学工具,以检测和订购流感感染以及生命最初几年经历的 疫苗接种。队列研究 努力在资格磨合期促进母婴对的发展,但也在一项深入的多年研究中成功地跟踪了那些符合资格的母婴对。
局限与证据强度
满足最终资格要求的参与者与未满足最终资格要求的参与者存在显着差异;登记的母亲往往年龄较大,有伴侣,是白人和/或非西班牙裔,有私人保险,家庭收入较高,受教育程度较高。由于入组时存在潜在的选择偏差,证据强度中等。

Original Paper The influence of early influenza exposures on immunity in infants and children: The US Influenza IMPRINT Cohort (Preprint)

Authors: Allison Burrell, Nancy Back, Chelsea Rohlfs, Sara Spranger, Shannon C. Conrey, Brendon White, Christina Quigley, Braxton Forde, Jens Wrammert, Jacob E. Kohlmeier
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Influenza vaccine effectiveness is variable, while influenza morbidity and mortality remain high. Immunologic imprinting for influenza has been described and may contribute to suboptimal vaccine performance. Unfortunately, critical data are lacking on the natural history of influenza in pregnancy and childhood. Appropriately designed maternal-birth cohorts can identify the earliest exposures to influenza and are necessary to address gaps in influenza epidemiology and immunology that currently hinder our ability to develop more broadly effective and durable vaccines. OBJECTIVE We describe the protocol for the Influenza IMPRINT Cohort (Immunological Memory to Prior INfluenza over Time), an intensive maternal-birth cohort, which aims to understand how early exposures to influenza antigens shape responses later in life. METHODS IMPRINT is a prospective birth cohort of healthy mothers and children in Cincinnati, Ohio. Mothers are enrolled in the third trimester and followed with their child for at least three years with an enrollment goal of 1,500 mother-child pairs. Starting in week 2 of life, weekly child nasal swabs and twice weekly symptom surveys are collected, regardless of the presence/absence of symptoms. Serum is collected from mothers at enrollment and each summer post-enrollment. Child blood samples are collected from the umbilical cord at delivery and from the child at week 6, every summer, and following influenza infection and vaccination. Whole blood, serum, plasma, and peripheral blood mononucleated cells are collected. Medical records are abstracted to capture clinical data on medically attended respiratory illness. Review of state registries and the medical record capture vaccine administration dates. Other collected specimens included stool, human milk, saliva, and cheek swabs. Epidemiologic, immunologic, and virologic studies are underway to determine how influenza exposures influence incidence, subclinical infection, symptomology, and immunologic responses to subsequent exposures. RESULTS Screening and enrollment began October 2019 and ended November 2025. In total, 1,624 mothers were provisionally enrolled in pregnancy and 1,324 met final eligibility criteria for study enrollment. Participant follow-up is on-going. Participants that met final eligibility requirements were significantly different from those that did not; enrolled mothers tended to be older, in a partnership, of white race and/or non-Hispanic ethnicity, have private insurance, higher household income, and more education. CONCLUSIONS IMPRINT integrates advanced methodologic, laboratory, and epidemiologic tools from a complimentary group of collaborators to detect and order influenza infections and vaccinations experienced in the first years of life. This cohort struggled to advance maternal-infant pairs through the eligibility run-in period but also demonstrated successful follow-up of those pairs that met eligibility in an intensive, multi-year study. IMPRINT provides a novel framework to improve understanding of the immunologic responses to primary influenza exposures and to describe how the initial exposure and the subsequent order of exposures impacts the influenza immune landscape. CLINICALTRIAL ClinicalTrials.gov ID NCT05436184

Question and background
BACKGROUND Influenza vaccine effectiveness is variable, while influenza morbidity and mortality remain high. Immunologic imprinting for influenza has been described and may contribute to suboptimal vaccine performance.
Design and population
IMPRINT is a prospective birth cohort of healthy mothers and children in Cincinnati, Ohio. Mothers are enrolled in the third trimester and followed with their child for at least three years with an enrollment goal of 1,500 mother-child pairs.
Core methods
Starting in week 2 of life, weekly child nasal swabs and twice weekly symptom surveys are collected, regardless of the presence/absence of symptoms. Serum is collected from mothers at enrollment and each summer post-enrollment. Child blood samples are collected from the umbilical cord at delivery and from the child at week 6, every summer, and following influenza infection and vaccination.
Main results
Screening and enrollment began October 2019 and ended November 2025. In total, 1,624 mothers were provisionally enrolled in pregnancy and 1,324 met final eligibility criteria for study enrollment. Participant follow-up is on-going.
Interpretation and novelty
IMPRINT provides a novel framework to improve understanding of the immunologic responses to primary influenza exposures and to describe how the initial exposure and the subsequent order of exposures impacts the influenza immune landscape.
Scientific and public-health significance
IMPRINT integrates advanced methodologic, laboratory, and epidemiologic tools from a complimentary group of collaborators to detect and order influenza infections and vaccinations experienced in the first years of life. This cohort struggled to advance maternal-infant pairs through the eligibility run-in period but also demonstrated successful follow-up of those pairs that met eligibility in an intensive, multi-year study.
Limitations and evidence strength
Participants that met final eligibility requirements were significantly different from those that did not; enrolled mothers tended to be older, in a partnership, of white race and/or non-Hispanic ethnicity, have private insurance, higher household income, and more education. The evidence strength is moderate due to the potential selection bias in enrollment.

肠道微生物群和代谢组的模式作为与系统性红斑狼疮中流感 疫苗 免疫原性相关的潜在生物标志物:横断面观察性试点研究

Patterns of the gut microbiota and metabolome as potential biomarkers associated with influenza vaccine immunogenicity in systemic lupus erythematosus: a cross-sectional observational pilot study
作者： Nathathai Kiewsanuan, Pintip Ngamjanyaporn, Prapaporn Pisitkun, Sunchai Payungporn, Vorthon Sawaswong, Prangwalai Chanchaem, Arnatchai Maiuthed, Supranee Thongpradit, Napong Lohaphuntrakun, Pichakorn Juntranggoor
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
在系统性红斑狼疮 (SLE) 中,流感 疫苗接种 的免疫原性通常受到损害,而肠道微生物群及其代谢输出在调节这种反应中的作用仍未得到充分研究。
设计与对象
2021 年 5 月至 2022 年 7 月期间,Ramathibodi 医院进行了一项横断面观察性试点研究,招募了 62 名 SLE 患者,他们接受了加强剂量或标准剂量的四价流感 疫苗。
核心方法
我们使用液相色谱-质谱分析了粪便 16 S rDNA 和代谢组学谱,以确定与血清转化相关的特征(使用血凝抑制测定法确定)。
主要结果
两组的总体肠道微生物群模式相似;然而,拟杆菌属与 A(H1N1) 和 甲型H3N2流感病毒 的血清转化显着相关 (P < 0.05)。代谢组学分析发现,血清转化者中 1,5-脱水-D-葡萄糖醇和 L-半胱氨酸上调,而无反应者中新蝶呤显着升高。
解释与创新
这些结果表明,肠道-免疫轴可能在 疫苗 反应中发挥调节作用,并可能提供潜在的非侵入性生物标志物,可以识别处于次优流感保护风险的患者。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果确定了特定的肠道微生物分类群和粪便代谢物,特别是 1,5-脱水-D-葡萄糖醇,它们与 SLE 患者的流感 疫苗 免疫原性显着相关,可能为个性化 疫苗接种 策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究为试点,样本量较小(n=62),横截面设计限制了因果推断;然而,肠道微生物群和代谢物的针对性分析为它们与 疫苗 反应的关联提供了适度的证据。

Patterns of the gut microbiota and metabolome as potential biomarkers associated with influenza vaccine immunogenicity in systemic lupus erythematosus: a cross-sectional observational pilot study

Authors: Nathathai Kiewsanuan, Pintip Ngamjanyaporn, Prapaporn Pisitkun, Sunchai Payungporn, Vorthon Sawaswong, Prangwalai Chanchaem, Arnatchai Maiuthed, Supranee Thongpradit, Napong Lohaphuntrakun, Pichakorn Juntranggoor
Original abstract (English)
Immunogenicity to influenza vaccination is often impaired in systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE). The role of the gut microbiota and its metabolic output in modulating this response remains underexplored. This study is the first to explore gut microbiota patterns and metabolites in response to influenza vaccination in patients with SLE. A cross-sectional observational pilot study was conducted at Ramathibodi Hospital between May 2021 and July 2022. Participants with SLE who had received either a boosted dose or a standard dose of a quadrivalent influenza vaccine were enrolled. We analyzed fecal 16 S rDNA and metabolomic profiles using liquid chromatography–mass spectrometry to identify signatures associated with seroconversion (determined using the hemagglutination inhibition assay). A total of 62 participants with SLE participated in the study, and 61 fecal samples were submitted for metabolomic analysis. Most participants were female (58/62, 93.6%). The median ages in the standard and booster dose groups were 35.5 (interquartile range 28–42.5) and 34 (25–43) years, respectively ( P = 0.878). The overall gut microbiota patterns were similar in both groups; however, the genus Bacteroides was significantly associated with seroconversion to A(H1N1) and A(H3N2) ( P < 0.05). Metabolomic analysis identified upregulation of 1,5-anhydro-D-glucitol and L-cysteic acid in seroconverters, whereas neopterin was significantly elevated in nonresponders, furthermore, several bacterial taxa were correlated with these metabolites. The level of immunosuppressive treatment and the use of antimicrobials for prophylaxis against opportunistic infections affected influenza immunogenicity in this study population. Some metabolites also appeared to be linked to vaccine immunogenicity, although the underlying mechanism is unclear. Our findings identify specific gut microbial taxa and fecal metabolites, particularly 1,5-anhydro-D-glucitol, which are significantly associated with influenza vaccine immunogenicity in SLE patients. These results suggest that the gut–immune axis may play a modulatory role in vaccine responses and may provide potential non-invasive biomarkers that can identify patients at risk of suboptimal influenza protection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Immunogenicity to influenza vaccination is often impaired in systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), and the role of the gut microbiota and its metabolic output in modulating this response remains underexplored.
Design and population
A cross-sectional observational pilot study was conducted at Ramathibodi Hospital between May 2021 and July 2022, enrolling 62 participants with SLE who had received either a boosted dose or a standard dose of a quadrivalent influenza vaccine.
Core methods
We analyzed fecal 16 S rDNA and metabolomic profiles using liquid chromatography–mass spectrometry to identify signatures associated with seroconversion (determined using the hemagglutination inhibition assay).
Main results
The overall gut microbiota patterns were similar in both groups; however, the genus Bacteroides was significantly associated with seroconversion to A(H1N1) and A(H3N2) (P < 0.05). Metabolomic analysis identified upregulation of 1,5-anhydro-D-glucitol and L-cysteic acid in seroconverters, whereas neopterin was significantly elevated in nonresponders.
Interpretation and novelty
These results suggest that the gut–immune axis may play a modulatory role in vaccine responses and may provide potential non-invasive biomarkers that can identify patients at risk of suboptimal influenza protection.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings identify specific gut microbial taxa and fecal metabolites, particularly 1,5-anhydro-D-glucitol, which are significantly associated with influenza vaccine immunogenicity in SLE patients, potentially informing personalized vaccination strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is a pilot with a small sample size (n=62), and the cross-sectional design limits causal inference; however, the targeted analysis of gut microbiota and metabolites provides moderate evidence for their association with vaccine response.

用于流感研究的新型基因敲除小鼠

New knockout mice for influenza research
作者： Alexandra Le Bras, Le Bras A.
摘要中文翻译他们首先从蛋白质相互作用组研究和大规模荟萃分析中收集了 386 个候选基因。尽管分子机制仍不清楚,但这些发现为未来 宿主 治疗开发提供了经过验证的 宿主 靶点的宝贵资源。实验动物|第 55 卷 | 2026 年 8 月 | 285 285 https://doi.org/10.1038/s41684-026-01776-9 研究亮点实验室动物传染病用于流感研究的新型基因敲除小鼠由于病毒依赖宿主细胞提供复制和传播所需的能量和资源,因此广泛的研究致力于识别促进感染的宿主因子。尽管这些研究,包括大量的体外筛选,已经发现了数百个可能有助于病毒复制的人类基因,但它们并没有完全捕捉到体内感染的复杂性。为了解决这一限制,一组研究人员生成了一个 CRISPR-Cas9 基因敲除小鼠文库,并用它来识别影响体内甲型流感病毒感染易感性的 宿主 基因。为了优先考虑体内分析的候选基因,研究人员采用了多步骤筛选策略。在排除生存必需的基因后,他们将候选基因范围缩小到 290 个。接下来,他们在甲型流感病毒感染的小鼠 L929 细胞中进行了基于 siRNA 的功能筛选,鉴定了 148 个在病毒复制中具有潜在作用的基因。从这些候选者中,该团队成功产生了 84 个基因敲除小鼠品系。该团队用致死剂量的小鼠适应型大流行甲型H1N1流感病毒株MA-CA04对84个小鼠系进行了挑战。他们监测了 14 天的存活率和体重,并计算了相对于野生型 (WT) 对照的增强保护功效 (EPE)。使用 av 的阈值...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
由于病毒依赖于 宿主 细胞进行复制和传播,因此广泛的研究已经确定了促进感染的 宿主 因子,但这些研究并未完全捕获体内感染的复杂性。
设计与对象
一组研究人员生成了一个 CRISPR-Cas9 基因敲除小鼠文库,并用它来识别影响体内甲型流感病毒感染易感性的 宿主 基因。
核心方法
他们从蛋白质相互作用组研究和荟萃分析中汇编了 386 个候选基因,在排除必需基因后将列表缩小到 290 个,在甲型流感病毒感染的小鼠 L929 细胞中进行了基于 siRNA 的功能筛选,并产生了 84 个敲除小鼠系。
主要结果
在用致死剂量的小鼠适应的甲型H1N1流行性流感病毒株MA-CA04攻击84个小鼠品系后,他们鉴定出17个基因修饰的品系具有增强的感染抵抗力,其中Arhgef28和Lasp1修饰的小鼠在两性中都显示出强大的保护作用。
解释与创新
尽管分子机制仍不清楚,但这些发现为未来 宿主 治疗开发提供了经过验证的 宿主 靶标的宝贵资源,并揭示了细胞培养研究中不明显的性别特异性效应。
科研与公卫意义
确定的 宿主 因子可以为新型 抗病毒药物 疗法的开发提供信息,并强调在流感研究中考虑性别特异性反应的重要性。
局限与证据强度
尽管大规模体内筛选方法提供了可靠的数据,但由于使用了单一的小鼠适应菌株和不清楚的分子机制,该研究的证据强度中等。

New knockout mice for influenza research

Authors: Alexandra Le Bras, Le Bras A.
Original abstract (English)
They first compiled 386 candidate genes from a protein interactome study and a large-scale meta-analysis. Although the molecular mechanisms remain unclear, these findings provide a valuable resource of validated host targets for future antiviral therapeutic development. Lab Animal | Volume 55 | August 2026 | 285 285 https://doi.org/10.1038/s41684-026-01776-9 Research highlights lab animal Infectious disease New knockout mice for influenza research Because viruses depend on host cells for the energy and resources needed to replicate and spread, extensive research has been devoted to identifying host factors that facilitate infection. Although these studies, including numerous in vitro screens, have uncovered hundreds of human genes that may contribute to viral replication, they do not fully capture the complexity of infection in vivo. To address this limitation, a team of researchers generated a library of CRISPR-Cas9-engineered knock- out mice and used it to identify host genes that influence susceptibility to influenza A virus infection in vivo. To prioritize candidate genes for in vivo analysis, the researchers employed a multi-step screening strategy. After excluding genes essential for survival, they narrowed the list to 290 can- didates. Next, they conducted an siRNA-based functional screen in influenza A virus-infected mouse L929 cells, identifying 148 genes with potential roles in viral replication. From these candidates, the team successfully generated 84 knockout mouse lines. The team challenged the 84 mouse lines with a lethal dose of the mouse-adapted pan- demic influenza A(H1N1) strain MA-CA04. They monitored survival and body weight for 14 days and calculated enhanced protective efficacy (EPE) relative to wild-type (WT) controls. Using a threshold of an av...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Because viruses depend on host cells for replication and spread, extensive research has identified host factors that facilitate infection, but these studies do not fully capture the complexity of infection in vivo.
Design and population
A team of researchers generated a library of CRISPR-Cas9-engineered knockout mice and used it to identify host genes that influence susceptibility to influenza A virus infection in vivo.
Core methods
They compiled 386 candidate genes from a protein interactome study and meta-analysis, narrowed the list to 290 after excluding essential genes, conducted an siRNA-based functional screen in influenza A virus-infected mouse L929 cells, and generated 84 knockout mouse lines.
Main results
After challenging the 84 mouse lines with a lethal dose of the mouse-adapted pandemic influenza A(H1N1) strain MA-CA04, they identified 17 gene-modified lines with increased resistance to infection, with Arhgef28- and Lasp1-modified mice showing robust protection in both sexes.
Interpretation and novelty
Although the molecular mechanisms remain unclear, these findings provide a valuable resource of validated host targets for future antiviral therapeutic development and reveal sex-specific effects not apparent in cell culture studies.
Scientific and public-health significance
The identified host factors could inform the development of novel antiviral therapies and highlight the importance of considering sex-specific responses in influenza research.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence strength is moderate due to the use of a single mouse-adapted strain and the unclear molecular mechanisms, though the large-scale in vivo screening approach provides robust data.
