河南省儿童社区获得性肺炎病原谱、合并感染特征及重症风险分析

Analysis of the pathogen spectrum, co-infection characteristics, and risk of severe disease in community-acquired pneumonia among children in Henan province
作者： Lu Xiaojing, Shufang Li, Qiuyan Yang, Zhijie Zhu, Yanli Zhang, Qingrong Xu
摘要中文翻译目的分析河南省社区获得性肺炎(CAP)儿童重症肺炎的病原谱、合并感染特征及危险因素。方法本研究纳入2023年6月至2025年6月郑州大学第三附属医院收治的2149名CAP儿科住院患者,采用多重聚合酶链反应(PCR)对咽拭子样本进行11种呼吸道病原体检测。结合培养结果,分析病原谱和合并感染特征,并采用多元Logistic回归分析确定重症肺炎的危险因素。值得注意的是,咽拭子 PCR 阳性结果不能明确区分病原体感染与定植、既往感染或无症状携带;在数据解释过程中考虑到了这种固有的局限性。结果病原菌总体检出率为89.34%,以肺炎支原体(31.55%)和鼻病毒(31.13%)为主;细菌中,流感嗜血杆菌(3.77%)和肺炎链球菌(3.35%)最为常见。除乙型流感病毒和人类冠状病毒外,各病原体的年龄分布均存在显着差异( P < 0.05)。肺炎支原体检出率随着年龄的增长而显着升高,学龄儿童中肺炎支原体检出率达到58.5%。鼻病毒全年活跃(2024年10月达到峰值65.14%),而肺炎支原体检出率最高的是2023年10月(65.93%)。总体混合感染率为31.07%,主要由病毒-病毒或病毒-非典型病原体组合组成(21.49%),其中鼻病毒-肺炎支原体最为常见(8.52%)。重症肺炎的独立危险因素是基础疾病(OR = 4.031)和早产史(OR = 3.544),而病毒或非典型病原体合并感染或并发细菌感染并不会显着增加重症风险。结论 2023年6月至2025年6月,河南省儿童CAP病原谱以肺炎支原体和鼻病毒为主(HRV阳性无症状携带率较高,需谨慎解读),年龄分布明显。基础疾病和早产史是重症肺炎的主要危险因素。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在分析河南省社区获得性肺炎(CAP)儿童重症肺炎的病原谱、合并感染特征及危险因素。
设计与对象
本研究纳入了2023年6月至2025年6月郑州大学第三附属医院收治的2,149名CAP儿科住院患者。
核心方法
采用多重聚合酶链式反应(PCR)对咽拭子样本进行11种呼吸道病原菌检测,结合培养结果,分析病原谱和合并感染特征,并采用多元Logistic回归分析确定重症肺炎的危险因素。
主要结果
病原总体检出率为89.34%,以肺炎支原体(31.55%)、鼻病毒(31.13%)为主;细菌中,流感嗜血杆菌(3.77%)和肺炎链球菌(3.35%)最为常见。重症肺炎的独立危险因素是基础疾病(OR = 4.031)和早产史(OR = 3.544)。
解释与创新
作者解读,河南省儿童CAP病原菌谱以肺炎支原体和鼻病毒为主,年龄分布明显,基础疾病和早产史是重症肺炎的主要危险因素。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可为 监测 和儿童 CAP 的预防策略提供信息,特别是关注主要病原体和高危人群,例如有基础疾病或有早产史的人群。
局限与证据强度
证据强度中等,因为咽拭子 PCR 结果呈阳性不能明确地区分致病性感染与定植、既往感染或无症状携带,这一点在数据解释过程中已考虑在内。

Analysis of the pathogen spectrum, co-infection characteristics, and risk of severe disease in community-acquired pneumonia among children in Henan province

Authors: Lu Xiaojing, Shufang Li, Qiuyan Yang, Zhijie Zhu, Yanli Zhang, Qingrong Xu
Original abstract (English)
Objective To analyze the pathogen spectrum, co-infection characteristics, and risk factors for severe pneumonia in children with community-acquired pneumonia (CAP) in Henan Province. Methods This study included 2,149 pediatric inpatients with CAP admitted to the Third Affiliated Hospital of Zhengzhou University from June 2023 to June 2025. Throat swab samples were tested using multiplex polymerase chain reaction (PCR) for 11 respiratory pathogens. Combined with culture results, the pathogen spectrum and co-infection characteristics were analyzed, and risk factors for severe pneumonia were identified using multivariate logistic regression analysis. It should be noted that a positive throat-swab PCR result cannot unambiguously differentiate pathogenic infection from colonization, prior infection, or asymptomatic carriage; this inherent limitation was taken into account during data interpretation. Results The overall pathogen detection rate was 89.34%, with Mycoplasma pneumoniae (31.55%) and rhinovirus (31.13%) predominating; among bacteria, Haemophilus influenzae (3.77%) and Streptococcus pneumoniae (3.35%) were the most common. Except for influenza B virus and human coronavirus, significant differences were observed in the age distribution of each pathogen ( P < 0.05). The detection rate of M. pneumoniae increased significantly with age, reaching 58.5% in school-aged children. Rhinovirus was active throughout the year (peaking at 65.14% in October 2024), while M. pneumoniae had the highest detection rate in October 2023 (65.93%). The overall co-infection rate was 31.07%, consisting primarily of virus–virus or virus–atypical pathogen combinations (21.49%), with rhinovirus– M. pneumoniae being the most common (8.52%). Independent risk factors for severe pneumonia were underlying medical conditions (OR = 4.031) and a history of preterm birth (OR = 3.544), whereas co-infections with viruses or atypical pathogens, or concurrent bacterial infections, did not significantly increase the risk of severe disease. Conclusion From June 2023 to June 2025, the pathogen spectrum of CAP in children in Henan Province was dominated by M. pneumoniae and rhinovirus (though HRV positivity should be interpreted with caution given its high asymptomatic carriage rate), with a distinct age distribution. Underlying medical conditions and a history of preterm birth were the primary risk factors for severe pneumonia.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to analyze the pathogen spectrum, co-infection characteristics, and risk factors for severe pneumonia in children with community-acquired pneumonia (CAP) in Henan Province.
Design and population
This study included 2,149 pediatric inpatients with CAP admitted to the Third Affiliated Hospital of Zhengzhou University from June 2023 to June 2025.
Core methods
Throat swab samples were tested using multiplex polymerase chain reaction (PCR) for 11 respiratory pathogens, and combined with culture results, the pathogen spectrum and co-infection characteristics were analyzed, with risk factors for severe pneumonia identified using multivariate logistic regression analysis.
Main results
The overall pathogen detection rate was 89.34%, with Mycoplasma pneumoniae (31.55%) and rhinovirus (31.13%) predominating; among bacteria, Haemophilus influenzae (3.77%) and Streptococcus pneumoniae (3.35%) were the most common. Independent risk factors for severe pneumonia were underlying medical conditions (OR = 4.031) and a history of preterm birth (OR = 3.544).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the pathogen spectrum of CAP in children in Henan Province was dominated by Mycoplasma pneumoniae and rhinovirus, with a distinct age distribution, and that underlying medical conditions and a history of preterm birth were the primary risk factors for severe pneumonia.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform surveillance and prevention strategies for CAP in children, particularly focusing on the predominant pathogens and high-risk groups such as those with underlying medical conditions or a history of preterm birth.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the inherent limitation that a positive throat-swab PCR result cannot unambiguously differentiate pathogenic infection from colonization, prior infection, or asymptomatic carriage, which was taken into account during data interpretation.

通过缺陷工程 Cu 3− x P 进行高效上转换发光猝灭,用于双适体介导的三模式 H1N1 侧流免疫分析

High-efficiency upconversion luminescent quenching via defect-engineered Cu 3− x P for dual-aptamers-mediated tri-modal H1N1 lateral flow immunoassay
作者： Qi Liu, Shili Gai, Haijiang Gong, Zhen Wan, Jie Chen, Qingqing Wang, He Ding, Yueming Ren, Fengni Xie, Piaoping Yang
摘要中文翻译摘要 甲型流感病毒的早期 诊断 对于减少重症和 诊断 流感预防和控制至关重要。然而,传统的侧流免疫分析(LFIA)由于灵敏度低而受到限制,而发光LFIA在低目标浓度下缺乏显着的信号变化。在此,通过集成上转换纳米粒子、缺陷工程 Cu3−xP 和双适体,开发了发光猝灭 LFIA 平台,以通过协同机制解决上述瓶颈。密度泛函理论计算表明,在新型 Cu3−xP 猝灭剂中,Cu 空位在价带边缘诱导新的电子态并产生额外的电子跃迁路径,从而提高福斯特共振能量转移和上转换发光猝灭效率。因此,有效抑制测试线发光信号,建立灵敏的发光猝灭LFIA,对信号变化敏感。Cu3−xP 还对色度和光热信号表现出广泛的紫外可见 (UV-vis) 吸收。协同发光-比色-光热三模式分析平台实现了 H1N1 血凝素的检测限为 0.907 ng/mL。此外,通过分子对接验证的两个具有稳定且不重叠结合位点的适配体对于形成夹心结构至关重要,使得对H1N1的敏感性高于H7N9、H9N2、H6N1、H7N7和H5N1,并且具有良好的抗干扰能力。这项工作提供了一种可扩展的护理点流感检测策略,平衡了灵敏度和特异性,优化了 LFIA 进行现场病毒诊断。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
甲型流感病毒的早期 诊断 通过减少重症和 诊断 对流感预防和控制至关重要。
设计与对象
该研究开发了一种发光猝灭侧流免疫分析 (LFIA) 平台,使用上转换纳米粒子、缺陷工程 Cu3−xP 和双适体检测 H1N1。
核心方法
该平台集成了上转换纳米颗粒、缺陷工程 Cu3−xP 和双适体,通过福斯特共振能量转移实现发光猝灭,并通过分子对接验证适体结合位点。
主要结果
三模式分析平台对 H1N1 血凝素的检测限达到 0.907 ng/mL,对 H1N1 的灵敏度高于 H7N9、H9N2、H6N1、H7N7 和 H5N1。
解释与创新
作者将发光、比色法和光热信号的协同作用解释为一种可扩展的护理点流感检测策略,优化 LFIA 以进行现场病毒诊断。
科研与公卫意义
这项工作为流感提供了一种敏感且具体的护理点检测策略,改善了现场病毒诊断,并有可能通过早期 诊断 减少严重疾病和 诊断。
局限与证据强度
证据强度很高,因为该研究提出了一种新颖的三模式平台,具有低检测限和经过验证的特异性,尽管仅抽象的证据限制了对现实世界适用性的评估。

High-efficiency upconversion luminescent quenching via defect-engineered Cu 3− x P for dual-aptamers-mediated tri-modal H1N1 lateral flow immunoassay

Authors: Qi Liu, Shili Gai, Haijiang Gong, Zhen Wan, Jie Chen, Qingqing Wang, He Ding, Yueming Ren, Fengni Xie, Piaoping Yang
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Early diagnosis of Influenza A virus is critical for influenza prevention and control by reducing severe illness and mortality. However, conventional lateral flow immunoassay (LFIA) is limited by low sensitivity, and luminescent LFIA is short of significant signal changes at low target concentrations. Herein, a luminescence quenching LFIA platform is developed by integrating upconversion nanoparticles, defect engineered Cu3−xP, and dual aptamers to address above bottleneck via a synergistic mechanism. Density functional theory calculations indicate that in the new type Cu3−xP quencher, Cu vacancies induce new electronic states at the valence band edge and create extra electron transition pathway, which boost Förster Resonance Energy Transfer and upconversion luminescence quenching efficiency. Therefore, the test line luminescence signal is effectively suppressed to establish a sensitive luminescence quenching LFIA, which is sensitive to signal changes. Cu3−xP also exhibits broad ultraviolet–visible (UV–vis) absorption for colorimetric and photothermal signals. The synergy luminescence-colorimetry-photothermal tri-modal analytical platform achieves a limit of detection of 0.907 ng/mL for H1N1 hemagglutinin. Moreover, two selected aptamers with stable and non-overlapping binding sites verified by molecular docking are critical for forming a sandwich structure, enabling high sensitivity toward H1N1 over H7N9, H9N2, H6N1, H7N7, and H5N1, along with good anti-interference ability. This work provides a scalable point of care influenza detection strategy balancing sensitivity and specificity, optimizing LFIA for on site viral diagnostics.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Early diagnosis of Influenza A virus is critical for influenza prevention and control by reducing severe illness and mortality.
Design and population
The study developed a luminescence quenching lateral flow immunoassay (LFIA) platform for H1N1 detection using upconversion nanoparticles, defect-engineered Cu3−xP, and dual aptamers.
Core methods
The platform integrates upconversion nanoparticles, defect-engineered Cu3−xP, and dual aptamers to achieve luminescence quenching via Förster Resonance Energy Transfer, with molecular docking verifying aptamer binding sites.
Main results
The tri-modal analytical platform achieves a limit of detection of 0.907 ng/mL for H1N1 hemagglutinin, with high sensitivity toward H1N1 over H7N9, H9N2, H6N1, H7N7, and H5N1.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the synergy of luminescence, colorimetry, and photothermal signals as a scalable point-of-care influenza detection strategy optimizing LFIA for on-site viral diagnostics.
Scientific and public-health significance
This work provides a sensitive and specific point-of-care detection strategy for influenza, improving on-site viral diagnostics and potentially reducing severe illness and mortality through early diagnosis.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is high as the study presents a novel tri-modal platform with a low limit of detection and validated specificity, though abstract-only evidence limits assessment of real-world applicability.

Journal: Biochemical Society transactions  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-01  |  DOI: 10.1042/bst20260075  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 54, 8, 1021-1052
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

破译乙型流感病毒衍生肽及其通过 HLA I 类分子的呈递。

Deciphering influenza B virus-derived peptides and their presentation by HLA class I molecules.
作者： Janesha C. Maddumage, Georgia R. Dow, Lawton D. Murdolo, Samuel Liwei Leong, Demetra S.M. Chatzileontiadou, Emma J. Grant, Stephanie Gras
摘要中文翻译尽管流感 疫苗 已广泛使用,但流感仍然每年导致显着的发病率和 死亡率。疫苗 通常会诱导体液介导的针对快速突变表面糖蛋白的保护,因此需要每年更新和施用它们。相比之下,可以控制和清除病毒感染的CD8+ T细胞可以识别更保守的病毒表位。因此,人们对了解 CD8+ T 细胞对流感病毒的反应有很大兴趣,以开发未来的 疫苗 和治疗方法。尽管甲型流感病毒 (FLUAV) 和甲型流感病毒 (FLUBV) 在人类中共同传播,并且都导致季节性流行,但有关 CD8+ T 细胞对 FLUBV 反应的数据有限。这种知识差距涵盖了免疫学和分子学方面的见解。在本综述中,我们总结了目前对 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位在细胞和分子水平上的了解,并与 FLUAV 进行了比较。总的来说,这凸显了有关 FLUBV 的可用数据有限,尽管它在人类流感感染中发挥着重要作用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注乙型流感病毒 (FLUBV) 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位及其 HLA I 类分子的呈递,并将其与甲型流感病毒 (FLUAV) 进行比较。中心问题解决了对 FLUBV 特异性 CD8+ T 细胞反应的有限的免疫学和分子学见解,尽管它在人类流感感染中发挥着重要作用。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一篇叙述性综合文章,总结了细胞和分子水平上 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位的当前知识。没有报告系统的检索策略、数据库或研究计数;它依赖于现有文献来比较 FLUBV 与 FLUAV。
共识与结论
人们一致认为,FLUBV 对 季节性流感 流行有显着贡献,但与 FLUAV 相比,其研究还不够。CD8+ T 细胞识别保守的病毒表位,使其成为未来 疫苗 和治疗的有希望的靶标,但 FLUBV 特异性数据仍然有限。
争议与缺口
关键证据差距包括关于 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位、其 HLA I 类呈现和免疫机制的数据有限。这些知识涵盖细胞和分子水平,强调需要对 FLUBV 特异性 免疫应答 进行有针对性的研究。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先表征 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位及其 HLA 呈现,为广泛保护性 疫苗 和治疗药物的开发提供信息。解决这些差距可以通过利用保守的 T 细胞表位来改善流感控制策略。

Deciphering influenza B virus-derived peptides and their presentation by HLA class I molecules.

Authors: Janesha C. Maddumage, Georgia R. Dow, Lawton D. Murdolo, Samuel Liwei Leong, Demetra S.M. Chatzileontiadou, Emma J. Grant, Stephanie Gras
Original abstract (English)
Despite influenza vaccines being widely available, influenza still causes significant morbidity and mortality annually. Vaccines typically induce humoral-mediated protection against rapidly mutating surface glycoproteins, necessitating that they be updated and administered each year. In contrast, CD8+ T cells, which can control and clear viral infections, can recognise more conserved viral epitopes. Therefore, there is considerable interest in understanding CD8+ T cell responses to influenza virus for the development of future vaccines and therapeutics. Although Alphainfluenzavirus influenzae (FLUAV) and Betainfluenzavirus influenzae (FLUBV) co-circulate in humans and both contribute to seasonal epidemics, there is limited data regarding CD8+ T cell responses to FLUBV. This knowledge gap spans both immunological and molecular insights. In the present review, we summarise the current knowledge of FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes at both cellular and molecular levels, in comparison with FLUAV. Collectively, this highlights the limited data available on FLUBV, despite its significant role in human influenza infections.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on influenza B virus (FLUBV)-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes and their presentation by HLA class I molecules, comparing them with influenza A virus (FLUAV). The central question addresses the limited immunological and molecular insights into FLUBV-specific CD8+ T cell responses, despite its significant role in human influenza infections.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis summarizing current knowledge of FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes at cellular and molecular levels. No systematic search strategy, database, or study count is reported; it relies on existing literature to compare FLUBV with FLUAV.
Consensus and conclusions
There is consensus that FLUBV contributes significantly to seasonal influenza epidemics but has been understudied compared to FLUAV. CD8+ T cells recognize conserved viral epitopes, making them promising targets for future vaccines and therapeutics, yet FLUBV-specific data remain limited.
Controversies and gaps
Key evidence gaps include limited data on FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes, their HLA class I presentation, and immunological mechanisms. This knowledge spans both cellular and molecular levels, highlighting a need for targeted research on FLUBV-specific immune responses.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize characterizing FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes and their HLA presentation to inform the development of broadly protective vaccines and therapeutics. Addressing these gaps could improve influenza control strategies by leveraging conserved T cell epitopes.

甲型流感病毒感染中的乳酰化:当前证据、知识差距和未来前景。

Lactylation in influenza a virus infection: Current evidence, knowledge gaps, and future perspectives.
作者： Yu-Mei Chen Yan, Zhijun Li, Haiyan He, Moding Wu, Haolin Yang, Fanhua Wei
摘要中文翻译甲型流感病毒(IAV)是一种主要呼吸道病原体,可引起季节性流行和大流行,对公众健康和牲畜造成严重威胁。IAV 的高 突变 率导致 疫苗 错配和耐药变异,强调需要新的 抗病毒药物 策略。本综述探讨了乳酰化在 IAV-宿主 相互作用中的作用,重点关注三个核心问题:IAV 如何诱导乳酰化、乳酰化如何重塑 抗病毒药物 免疫以及 IAV 如何利用乳酰化进行免疫逃避。主要发现包括有效复制所需的病毒 vRNP 成分的乳酰化以及 宿主 脱乙酰酶 SIRT1,后者通过去除这些乳酰基来抑制 IAV 复制。IAV 通过下调 SIRT1 表达来抵消这种防御。乳酰化还调节 cGAS-STING、RLR-MAVS 和 IFN 信号通路。讨论了针对乳酸代谢和 SIRT1 的治疗潜力。了解 IAV 感染中的乳酰化可能为 抗病毒药物 药物开发开辟新途径。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述针对季节性人类甲型和乙型流感病毒,特别是 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒 和 B/Victoria 谱系,以检查乳酰化作为感染过程中 宿主 病毒相互作用机制。核心问题是乳酰化如何影响甲型流感病毒 (IAV) 复制、抗病毒药物 免疫和免疫逃避。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一种叙述性综合,不报告数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究选择方法。它综合了 IAV-宿主 相互作用中乳酰化的证据,而无需进行系统或荟萃分析审查。
共识与结论
病毒 vRNP 成分的乳酰化是 IAV 有效复制所必需的,而 宿主 脱乙酰酶 SIRT1 通过去除乳酰基来抑制 IAV 复制。IAV 下调 SIRT1 表达以抵消这种 抗病毒药物 防御。乳酰化还调节 cGAS-STING、RLR-MAVS 和 IFN 信号通路。
争议与缺口
该综述没有提供有关特定病毒或 宿主 蛋白的乳酰化如何直接改变 抗病毒药物 途径的定量数据或机制细节。IAV 感染中乳酰化和其他翻译后修饰之间的相互作用仍未解决。
科研与实践启示
提出以乳酸代谢和 SIRT1 活性为目标作为抑制 IAV 复制的治疗策略。需要进一步的研究来阐明 IAV 感染期间乳酰化调节 抗病毒药物 免疫和免疫逃避的精确分子机制。

Lactylation in influenza a virus infection: Current evidence, knowledge gaps, and future perspectives.

Authors: Yu-Mei Chen Yan, Zhijun Li, Haiyan He, Moding Wu, Haolin Yang, Fanhua Wei
Original abstract (English)
Influenza A virus (IAV) is a major respiratory pathogen causing seasonal epidemics and pandemics, posing serious threats to public health and livestock. The high mutation rate of IAV leads to vaccine mismatches and drug-resistant variants, underscoring the need for novel antiviral strategies. This review examines the role of lactylation in IAV-host interactions, focusing on three core questions: how IAV induces lactylation, how lactylation reshapes antiviral immunity, and how IAV exploits lactylation for immune evasion. Key findings include lactylation of viral vRNP components required for efficient replication and the host deacetylase SIRT1, which suppresses IAV replication by removing these lactyl groups. IAV counteracts this defense by downregulating SIRT1 expression. Lactylation also regulates cGAS-STING, RLR-MAVS, and IFN signaling pathways. The therapeutic potential of targeting lactate metabolism and SIRT1 is discussed. Understanding lactylation in IAV infection may open new avenues for antiviral drug development.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review targets seasonal human influenza A and B viruses, specifically A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria lineage, to examine lactylation as a host-virus interaction mechanism during infection. The central question is how lactylation influences influenza A virus (IAV) replication, antiviral immunity, and immune evasion.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis that does not report databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study selection methods. It synthesizes evidence on lactylation in IAV-host interactions without conducting a systematic or meta-analytic review.
Consensus and conclusions
Lactylation of viral vRNP components is required for efficient IAV replication, while host deacetylase SIRT1 suppresses IAV replication by removing lactyl groups. IAV downregulates SIRT1 expression to counteract this antiviral defense. Lactylation also modulates cGAS-STING, RLR-MAVS, and IFN signaling pathways.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not provide quantitative data or mechanistic details on how lactylation of specific viral or host proteins directly alters antiviral pathways. The interplay between lactylation and other post-translational modifications in IAV infection remains unresolved.
Research and practice implications
Targeting lactate metabolism and SIRT1 activity is proposed as a therapeutic strategy to inhibit IAV replication. Further research is needed to elucidate the precise molecular mechanisms by which lactylation regulates antiviral immunity and immune evasion during IAV infection.

人类对 H5N1 禽流感已有的免疫和交叉反应免疫:对大流行风险和 疫苗 策略的影响。

Pre-existing and Cross-Reactive Immunity to Avian Influenza H5N1 in Humans: Implications for Pandemic Risk and Vaccine Strategies.
作者： Iván Sanz-Muñoz, Carlos J Ciria-Gil, Marta Hernandez, Cesar Santiago, Ahmed M Elsayed, Jose M Erios, Luis Martinez-Sobrido, Aitor Nogales
摘要中文翻译由于甲型流感病毒(IAV)的连续传播,可能会出现具有高效人传人传播的新毒株,并导致未来的大流行。其中,高致病性禽流感(HPAI)H5N1 仍然是一个主要问题,因为它对野生动物、牲畜和人类健康有影响。在全球数百种鸟类和众多哺乳动物中检测到的 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 的广泛传播,凸显了病毒生态学和 传播 的重要变化,增加了其人畜共患和大流行的可能性。本综述总结了人类对 H5N1 交叉反应和交叉保护性免疫的当前证据,主要关注体液 免疫应答。我们检查了未接触过已知病毒的个体中是否存在预先存在的 H5N1 反应性抗体,并讨论了先前的 季节性流感 感染或 疫苗接种 可能如何促进其发展。特别关注针对血凝素 (HA) 保守区域(尤其是茎结构域)以及神经氨酸酶 (NA) 的抗体,它们可能提供异亚型保护。我们还评估了 季节性流感 疫苗 和感染诱导针对 H5N1 交叉反应的能力及其在部分保护或免疫启动中的潜在作用。最后,我们回顾了当前和新兴的 H5N1 疫苗接种 策略,包括佐剂和基于 mRNA 的平台,并确定了 监测、群体免疫评估和广泛保护性流感 疫苗 的开发的优先事项。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了人类对禽流感 H5N1 已有的免疫和交叉反应免疫力,重点关注体液 免疫应答 及其对 H5N1 提供部分保护或免疫启动的潜力,H5N1 是一种高致病性禽流感病毒,具有显着的人畜共患和大流行潜力。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了人类对 H5N1 的交叉反应性和交叉保护性免疫的证据,强调了体液 免疫应答 以及血凝素 (HA) 和神经氨酸酶 (NA) 的保守区域,特别是茎结构域。
共识与结论
该综述强调了在没有已知暴露情况的个体中存在预先存在的 H5N1 反应性抗体,可能是由先前的 季节性流感 感染或 疫苗接种 诱导的,并强调了针对保守 HA 茎和 NA 结构域的抗体在提供异亚型保护中的作用。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告不同人群中交叉反应性 免疫应答 的异质性程度、这种免疫的持久性或预先存在的 H5N1 反应性抗体发展的精确机制。
科研与实践启示
该审查确定了 监测 评估人群免疫力、开发广泛保护性流感 疫苗 以及评估当前和新兴 H5N1 疫苗接种 策略(包括佐剂和基于 mRNA 的平台)的优先事项。

Pre-existing and Cross-Reactive Immunity to Avian Influenza H5N1 in Humans: Implications for Pandemic Risk and Vaccine Strategies.

Authors: Iván Sanz-Muñoz, Carlos J Ciria-Gil, Marta Hernandez, Cesar Santiago, Ahmed M Elsayed, Jose M Erios, Luis Martinez-Sobrido, Aitor Nogales
Original abstract (English)
Due to the continuous evolution of Influenza A viruses (IAVs), novel strains with efficient human-to-human transmission may emerge and cause future pandemics. Among these, highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) H5N1 remains a major concern because of its impact on wildlife, livestock, and human health. The widespread circulation of H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b, detected in hundreds of bird species and numerous mammals worldwide, highlights important changes in viral ecology and transmission, increasing its zoonotic and pandemic potential. This review summarizes current evidence on cross-reactive and cross-protective immunity to H5N1 in humans, focusing primarily on humoral immune responses. We examine the presence of pre-existing H5N1-reactive antibodies in individuals without known exposure and discuss how previous seasonal influenza infection or vaccination may contribute to their development. Particular attention is given to antibodies targeting conserved regions of hemagglutinin (HA), especially the stalk domain, as well as neuraminidase (NA), which may provide heterosubtypic protection. We also evaluate the ability of seasonal influenza vaccines and infections to induce cross-reactive responses against H5N1 and their potential role in partial protection or immune priming. Finally, we review current and emerging H5N1 vaccination strategies, including adjuvanted and mRNA-based platforms, and identify priorities for surveillance, population immunity assessment, and the development of broadly protective influenza vaccines.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines pre-existing and cross-reactive immunity to avian influenza H5N1 in humans, focusing on humoral immune responses and their potential to confer partial protection or immune priming against H5N1, a highly pathogenic avian influenza virus with significant zoonotic and pandemic potential.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes evidence on cross-reactive and cross-protective immunity to H5N1 in humans, emphasizing humoral immune responses and conserved regions of hemagglutinin (HA) and neuraminidase (NA), particularly the stalk domain.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights the presence of pre-existing H5N1-reactive antibodies in individuals without known exposure, likely induced by prior seasonal influenza infection or vaccination, and underscores the role of antibodies targeting conserved HA stalk and NA domains in providing heterosubtypic protection.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report the extent of heterogeneity in cross-reactive immune responses across populations, the durability of such immunity, or the precise mechanisms underlying the development of pre-existing H5N1-reactive antibodies.
Research and practice implications
The review identifies priorities for surveillance to assess population immunity, the development of broadly protective influenza vaccines, and the evaluation of current and emerging H5N1 vaccination strategies, including adjuvanted and mRNA-based platforms.

针对 COVID-19 大流行的非药物干预措施对流感负担的影响 - 系统评价和荟萃分析。

Impact of Non-Pharmaceutical Interventions Targeted at COVID-19 Pandemic on Influenza Burden-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.
作者： Laura-Inés Böhler, Lisa Koeppel, Stefan Fabian Weber, Mary Gaeddert, Katharina Thielemann, Kerstin Glaser, Horeya M. Ismail, Ulrich Reinacher, Veronika K. Jaeger, Julia Böhnke
摘要中文翻译摘要 季节性流感 给全球健康带来了巨大的负担。COVID-19 大流行伴随着广泛的非药物干预措施 (NPI),严重扰乱了呼吸道病毒的循环,为评估其更广泛的流行病学影响提供了独特的机会。本研究旨在量化大流行前和大流行期间流感负担的变化。在 RESPINOW 项目中,我们按照 PRISMA 指南进行了系统审查。报告大流行之前和期间的绝对流感病例数的研究也包括在内。使用改进的 NHLBI 工具进行前后研究的数据提取和质量评估。流感病例通过报告期长度进行标准化,并使用 发病率 比率来估计相对变化。亚组分析探讨了年龄、环境、半球、人类发展指数、流感 传播 区域、世界卫生组织区域和病毒株。在 20,676 项筛选记录中,纳入了来自 98 个国家的 115 项研究。在全球范围内,流感 发病率 在大流行爆发后下降了 -92%(95% CI:-94 至 -90)。下降幅度因地域而异,从一些国家的接近消除到较温和的下降(例如韩国:-61%,95% CI:-79 至 -26)。所有 传播 区域和世卫组织区域均观察到下降,其中高收入国家(−96%、95% CI:-98 至 -94)观察到的下降幅度明显大于低收入国家(−82%、95% CI:-88 至 -73)。与 18-64 岁成年人(-80%,95% CI:-90 至 -63)相比,幼儿(< 6 岁:-66%,95% CI:-79 至 -45)的年龄特异性下降幅度较小。甲型流感似乎比乙型流感下降得更厉害。COVID-19 大流行与流感 发病率 史无前例的全球减少有关。数据的局限性和对可靠流行病学指标的需求凸显了加强、综合的全球 监测 为未来大流行应对措施提供信息的重要性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本系统综述和荟萃分析重点关注人群中的 季节性流感 病毒(甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒、B/Victoria 和历史 B/Yamagata)。核心问题是量化大流行前和大流行期间流感负担的变化,特别检查与 COVID-19 相关的非药物干预措施 (NPI) 对流感 发病率 的影响。
证据基础与方法
该研究遵循 PRISMA 指南并在 RESPINOW 项目内进行。它包括来自 98 个国家的 115 项研究,报告了大流行之前和期间的绝对流感病例数。数据提取和质量评估使用修改后的 NHLBI 工具进行前后研究。流感病例通过报告期长度进行标准化,并使用 发病率 比率来估计相对变化。亚组分析探讨了年龄、环境、半球、人类发展指数、流感 传播 区域、世界卫生组织区域和病毒株。
共识与结论
COVID-19大流行与全球流感发病率史无前例的下降有关,下降了-92%(95% CI:-94至-90)。所有 传播 区域和世卫组织区域均观察到下降,与低收入国家(−82%、95% CI:-88 至 -73)相比,高收入国家(−96%、95% CI:-98 至 -94)下降幅度更大。幼儿(<6岁:-66%,95% CI:-79至-45)的年龄特异性下降幅度小于18-64岁成人(-80%,95% CI:-90至-63)。甲型流感似乎比乙型流感下降得更厉害。
争议与缺口
该研究指出,流感减少情况存在显着的地理差异,从一些国家接近消除到较温和的下降(例如韩国:-61%,95% CI:-79 至 -26)。强调了数据的局限性和对可靠流行病学指标的需求,特别是关于不同环境下 NPI 实施和合规性的可变性。该研究没有解决年龄或病毒类型不同影响背后的潜在机制。
科研与实践启示
研究结果强调了加强、一体化的全球 监测 为未来大流行应对措施提供信息的重要性。需要强有力的流行病学指标来更好地了解 NPI 对呼吸道病毒的影响并制定有针对性的干预措施。未来的研究应探讨导致大流行期间流感负担地理和人口变化的因素。

Impact of Non-Pharmaceutical Interventions Targeted at COVID-19 Pandemic on Influenza Burden-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

Authors: Laura-Inés Böhler, Lisa Koeppel, Stefan Fabian Weber, Mary Gaeddert, Katharina Thielemann, Kerstin Glaser, Horeya M. Ismail, Ulrich Reinacher, Veronika K. Jaeger, Julia Böhnke
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Seasonal influenza imposes a substantial global health burden. The COVID‐19 pandemic, accompanied by widespread non‐pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs), profoundly disrupted respiratory virus circulation, offering a unique opportunity to assess their broader epidemiological impact. This study aimed to quantify changes in influenza burden between the pre‐pandemic and intra‐pandemic periods. Within the RESPINOW project, we conducted a systematic review following PRISMA guidelines. Studies reporting absolute influenza case counts before and during the pandemic were included. Data extraction and quality assessment were performed using a modified NHLBI tool for before–after studies. Influenza cases were normalized by reporting period length, and relative changes were estimated using incidence rate ratios. Subgroup analyses explored age, setting, hemisphere, human development index, influenza transmission zones, WHO regions, and viral strains. Of 20,676 screened records, 115 studies from 98 countries were included. Globally, influenza incidence declined by −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90) following the onset of the pandemic. Reductions varied geographically, ranging from near‐elimination in several countries to more modest declines (e.g., South Korea: −61%, 95% CI: −79 to −26). Decreases were observed across all transmission zones and WHO regions, with descriptively larger reductions observed in high‐income countries (−96%, 95% CI: −98 to −94) than in low‐income settings (−82%, 95% CI: −88 to −73). Age‐specific declines appeared smaller among young children (< 6 years: −66%, 95% CI: −79 to −45) compared with adults aged 18–64 years (−80%, 95% CI: −90 to −63). Influenza A appeared to decline more strongly than influenza B. The COVID‐19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence. Data limitations and the need for robust epidemiological indicators highlight the importance of strengthened, integrated global surveillance to inform future pandemic responses.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review and meta-analysis focused on seasonal influenza viruses (A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), B/Victoria, and historical B/Yamagata) in human populations. The central question was to quantify changes in influenza burden between pre-pandemic and intra-pandemic periods, specifically examining the impact of COVID-19-related non-pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs) on influenza incidence.
Evidence base and method
The study followed PRISMA guidelines and was conducted within the RESPINOW project. It included 115 studies from 98 countries reporting absolute influenza case counts before and during the pandemic. Data extraction and quality assessment used a modified NHLBI tool for before-after studies. Influenza cases were normalized by reporting period length, and relative changes were estimated using incidence rate ratios. Subgroup analyses explored age, setting, hemisphere, human development index, influenza transmission zones, WHO regions, and viral strains.
Consensus and conclusions
The COVID-19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence, with a decline of −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90). Reductions were observed across all transmission zones and WHO regions, with larger decreases in high-income countries (−96%, 95% CI: −98 to −94) compared to low-income settings (−82%, 95% CI: −88 to −73). Age-specific declines were smaller among young children (<6 years: −66%, 95% CI: −79 to −45) than in adults aged 18–64 years (−80%, 95% CI: −90 to −63). Influenza A appeared to decline more strongly than influenza B.
Controversies and gaps
The study noted significant geographic variation in influenza reductions, ranging from near-elimination in some countries to more modest declines (e.g., South Korea: −61%, 95% CI: −79 to −26). Data limitations and the need for robust epidemiological indicators were highlighted, particularly regarding the variability in NPI implementation and compliance across settings. The study did not address potential mechanisms behind differential impacts by age or viral type.
Research and practice implications
The findings underscore the importance of strengthened, integrated global surveillance to inform future pandemic responses. There is a need for robust epidemiological indicators to better understand the impact of NPIs on respiratory viruses and to develop targeted interventions. Future research should explore the factors contributing to geographic and demographic variations in influenza burden during the pandemic.

CD4 T 细胞库和免疫记忆机制在 疫苗接种 和感染免疫中的作用。

The Role of CD4 T Cell Repertoire and Immune Memory Mechanisms in Vaccination and Infection Immunity.
作者： Masato Kubo
摘要中文翻译CD4 + T 细胞通过功能不同的子集协调针对呼吸道病毒的适应性免疫,但 疫苗 和感染诱导的反应之间的质量差异仍不完全清楚。本综述总结了关于 CD4 + T 细胞亚群如何影响 SARS-CoV-2 和流感病毒免疫的程度、广度和持久性的当前证据。mRNA 疫苗 引发有效的 Th1/Tfh1 偏向反应,产生高亲和力中和抗体和具有干细胞记忆特性的细胞毒性 CD4 + T 细胞 (CD4-CTL)。然而,这种强烈的 I 型偏见可能是以长期维持体液记忆为代价的。自然感染还在呼吸道粘膜表面建立了组织驻留记忆 CD4 + T 细胞 (CD4 TRM),为当前肌肉注射 疫苗 无法有效重演提供前线防御。至关重要的是,这种 T 细胞免疫被证明比针对关注变体的中和抗体更具弹性,因为来自祖先菌株的许多记忆 T 细胞表位在连续变体中是保守的,通过先前的季节性冠状病毒暴露或流感感染引发的预先存在的交叉反应性 CD4 + T 细胞进一步增强了这种稳健性。这些观察结果表明,疫苗 的设计发生了根本性转变,转向粘膜递送和保守表位抗原,以实现持久、广泛的交叉反应保护。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述探讨了 CD4+ T 细胞库和免疫记忆机制在 疫苗接种 和呼吸道病毒感染免疫中的作用,特别关注 SARS-CoV-2 和流感病毒免疫。
证据基础与方法
本综述总结了关于 CD4+ T 细胞亚群如何影响 SARS-CoV-2 和流感病毒免疫的程度、广度和持久性的当前证据。
共识与结论
mRNA 疫苗 引发有效的 Th1/Tfh1 偏向反应,产生高亲和力中和抗体和具有干细胞记忆特性的细胞毒性 CD4+ T 细胞 (CD4-CTL)。自然感染还会在呼吸道粘膜表面建立组织驻留记忆 CD4+ T 细胞 (CD4 TRM)。事实证明,T 细胞免疫比针对关注变体的中和抗体更具弹性,因为许多记忆 T 细胞表位在变体中是保守的。
争议与缺口
mRNA 疫苗 中强烈的 I 型偏倚可能是以体液记忆的长期维持为代价的。目前的肌肉注射 疫苗 无法有效地重现自然感染建立的呼吸道粘膜表面的组织驻留记忆 CD4+ T 细胞。
科研与实践启示
这些观察结果表明,疫苗 的设计发生了根本性转变,转向粘膜递送和保守表位抗原,以实现持久、广泛的交叉反应保护。

The Role of CD4 T Cell Repertoire and Immune Memory Mechanisms in Vaccination and Infection Immunity.

Authors: Masato Kubo
Original abstract (English)
CD4 + T cells orchestrate adaptive immunity against respiratory viruses through functionally distinct subsets, yet the qualitative differences between vaccine- and infection-induced responses remain incompletely understood. This review summarizes current evidence on how CD4 + T cell subsets shape the magnitude, breadth, and durability of immunity to SARS-CoV-2 and influenza virus. mRNA vaccines elicit a potent Th1/Tfh1-biased response that generates high-affinity neutralizing antibodies and cytotoxic CD4 + T cells (CD4-CTLs) with stem-cell memory properties. However, this strong type I bias may come at the cost of long-term maintenance of humoral memory. Natural infection additionally establishes tissue-resident memory CD4 + T cells (CD4 TRMs) at respiratory mucosal surfaces, providing a frontline defense that current intramuscular vaccines fail to recapitulate efficiently. Crucially, this T cell immunity proves far more resilient than neutralizing antibodies against Variants of Concern, as many memory T cell epitopes from the ancestral strain are conserved across successive variants, a robustness further reinforced by pre-existing cross-reactive CD4 + T cells primed through prior seasonal coronavirus exposure or influenza infection. These observations argue for a fundamental shift in vaccine design toward mucosal delivery and conserved-epitope antigens to achieve durable, broadly cross-reactive protection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the role of CD4+ T cell repertoire and immune memory mechanisms in vaccination and infection immunity against respiratory viruses, specifically focusing on SARS-CoV-2 and influenza virus immunity.
Evidence base and method
This review summarizes current evidence on how CD4+ T cell subsets shape the magnitude, breadth, and durability of immunity to SARS-CoV-2 and influenza virus.
Consensus and conclusions
mRNA vaccines elicit a potent Th1/Tfh1-biased response that generates high-affinity neutralizing antibodies and cytotoxic CD4+ T cells (CD4-CTLs) with stem-cell memory properties. Natural infection additionally establishes tissue-resident memory CD4+ T cells (CD4 TRMs) at respiratory mucosal surfaces. T cell immunity proves more resilient than neutralizing antibodies against Variants of Concern, as many memory T cell epitopes are conserved across variants.
Controversies and gaps
The strong type I bias in mRNA vaccines may come at the cost of long-term maintenance of humoral memory. Current intramuscular vaccines fail to efficiently recapitulate tissue-resident memory CD4+ T cells at respiratory mucosal surfaces established by natural infection.
Research and practice implications
These observations argue for a fundamental shift in vaccine design toward mucosal delivery and conserved-epitope antigens to achieve durable, broadly cross-reactive protection.

推进流感预防:暴露前预防 (PrEP) 的案例

Advancing Influenza Prevention: The Case for Pre-Exposure Prophylaxis (PrEP)
作者： Hanumantha Rao Paritala, Luis Mier-y-Terán-Romero, Ramya Natarajan, Peter Adams, Cassandra Spector, Katherine Topf, Ashwin Kadambi, Jane Falvey, Mark J. Lamias, David P. Durham
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查重点关注 传播 病毒对国家健康安全的重大威胁,特别是对弱势群体而言,并探讨暴露前预防 (PrEP) 作为补充策略,以在现有控制措施不足时减少 传播 和疾病严重程度。
证据基础与方法
The manuscript outlines the Biomedical Advanced Research and Development Authority's (BARDA's) target product profile (TPP) for a long-acting influenza PrEP product, reviews the current development landscape, and models the potential impact of early PrEP deployment using agent-based modeling in a synthetic population of 19.5 million people across pandemic scenarios resembling the 1918, 1968, and 2009 influenza pandemics.
共识与结论
临床研究支持 PrEP 疗法作为一种有前途的补充策略,可减少流感 传播 和疾病严重程度。模拟显示,早期部署有效率为 70% 的 PrEP 可减少累积感染和峰值感染,推迟流行高峰,并在传播性较小的流行病中提供最大益处;在 40-50% 的覆盖率下,PrEP 完全缓解了类似 2009 年的大流行,并在类似 1918 年和 1968 年的情况下大幅减少了 传播。
争议与缺口
保护方面的差距在大流行早期尤其明显,此时可能尚未获得良好匹配的 疫苗,以及在无法做出最佳 疫苗 反应的人群中。TPP 定义了有效 PrEP 方案的关键特征,但审查并未解决超出这些限制的潜在争议或具体证据差距。
科研与实践启示
将PrEP纳入流感预防策略可以改善对病毒传播的控制,加强对高危人群的保护,并显着减少季节性和大流行性流感对公众健康的总体影响。TPP 强调强大的安全性、直接作用的 抗病毒药物 机制、症状感染风险至少降低 70%,以及单剂量、全季保护以优化依从性。

Advancing Influenza Prevention: The Case for Pre-Exposure Prophylaxis (PrEP)

Authors: Hanumantha Rao Paritala, Luis Mier-y-Terán-Romero, Ramya Natarajan, Peter Adams, Cassandra Spector, Katherine Topf, Ashwin Kadambi, Jane Falvey, Mark J. Lamias, David P. Durham
Original abstract (English)
Influenza remains a significant national health security threat, particularly for vulnerable populations, as existing control measures do not fully mitigate its impact. This gap in protection is especially pronounced early in a pandemic when a well-matched vaccine may not yet be available, as well as in populations unable to mount an optimal vaccine response. Clinical studies support pre-exposure prophylaxis (PrEP) therapeutics as a promising complementary strategy to reduce influenza transmission and disease severity, potentially easing the strain on healthcare systems during outbreaks. This manuscript outlines the Biomedical Advanced Research and Development Authority’s (BARDA’s) target product profile (TPP) for a long-acting influenza PrEP product, reviews the current development landscape, and models the potential impact of early PrEP product deployment using agent-based modeling in a synthetic population of 19.5 million people across pandemic scenarios resembling the 1918, 1968, and 2009 influenza pandemics. Simulations showed that early deployment of a 70%–effective PrEP reduced cumulative and peak infections, delayed the epidemic peak, and provided the greatest benefit in less transmissible pandemics; at 40–50% coverage, PrEP fully mitigated a 2009-like pandemic and substantially reduced transmission in 1918- and 1968-like scenarios. The TPP defines key characteristics of an effective PrEP option for seasonal and pandemic influenza that (1) demonstrates a strong safety and tolerability profile across all populations; (2) targets a direct-acting antiviral mechanism of action; (3) reduces the relative risk of symptomatic influenza infection by at least 70% in an unvaccinated population and (4) provides single-dose, season-long protection to optimize patient adherence. In this context, integrating PrEP into influenza prevention strategies could improve control of virus spread, strengthen protection for high-risk groups, and significantly reduce the overall public health impact of seasonal and pandemic influenza.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on seasonal influenza viruses as a significant national health security threat, particularly for vulnerable populations, and explores pre-exposure prophylaxis (PrEP) as a complementary strategy to reduce transmission and disease severity when existing control measures are insufficient.
Evidence base and method
The manuscript outlines the Biomedical Advanced Research and Development Authority's (BARDA's) target product profile (TPP) for a long-acting influenza PrEP product, reviews the current development landscape, and models the potential impact of early PrEP deployment using agent-based modeling in a synthetic population of 19.5 million people across pandemic scenarios resembling the 1918, 1968, and 2009 influenza pandemics.
Consensus and conclusions
Clinical studies support PrEP therapeutics as a promising complementary strategy to reduce influenza transmission and disease severity. Simulations showed that early deployment of a 70%–effective PrEP reduced cumulative and peak infections, delayed the epidemic peak, and provided the greatest benefit in less transmissible pandemics; at 40–50% coverage, PrEP fully mitigated a 2009-like pandemic and substantially reduced transmission in 1918- and 1968-like scenarios.
Controversies and gaps
The gap in protection is especially pronounced early in a pandemic when a well-matched vaccine may not yet be available, as well as in populations unable to mount an optimal vaccine response. The TPP defines key characteristics of an effective PrEP option, but the review does not address potential controversies or specific evidence gaps beyond these limitations.
Research and practice implications
Integrating PrEP into influenza prevention strategies could improve control of virus spread, strengthen protection for high-risk groups, and significantly reduce the overall public health impact of seasonal and pandemic influenza. The TPP emphasizes a strong safety profile, direct-acting antiviral mechanism, at least 70% reduction in symptomatic infection risk, and single-dose, season-long protection to optimize adherence.

RNA病毒反向遗传学技术的发展、应用及未来展望

Development, applications, and future prospects of RNA virus reverse genetics technology
作者： Yu Guo, Ting Xue, Jianhua Wang, Xueqiao Xie, Furui Cao, Yijun Zhang, huiting Wang, 吕冰清
摘要中文翻译病毒反向遗传学能够从克隆的 cDNA 中拯救感染性病毒粒子,并作为绘制病毒基因型-表型关系、剖析 RNA 病毒生命周期和开发 抗病毒药物 对策的核心技术。该综述系统总结了进化、RNA病毒反向遗传学的技术框架和优化策略,涵盖了基因组所有主要RNA类型的三种原型病毒:SARS-CoV-2(+ssRNA)、不分节段负义新城疫病毒(NDV)和节段负义甲型流感病毒。详细阐述了感染性克隆构建、多样化启动子系统、锤头/HDV核酶以及BAC和ISA等大型不稳定基因组的解决方案等核心模块。我们总结了其在疫苗开发、发病机制研究、病毒-宿主相互作用分析和高通量抗病毒药物筛选等方面不可替代的应用。目前的瓶颈包括救援效率低、cDNA遗传不稳定和生物安全隐患。我们还引入非传染性替代平台,建立三层抗病毒药物筛选管道。未来的进步将整合 CRISPR/Cas 编辑、标准化模块化工具、生物安全工程、人工智能和大数据。与以往关注单一病毒属的综述不同,该工作进行跨类型的横向比较,总结普遍的技术障碍和量身定制的优化,为基础病毒学、加速疫苗创新和精准抗病毒药物设计提供全面参考。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
综述重点关注RNA病毒反向遗传学技术,涵盖其发展、应用和未来前景。核心问题涉及了解该技术如何能够从克隆的 cDNA 中拯救感染性病毒颗粒,及其在绘制病毒基因型-表型关系、剖析 RNA 病毒生命周期和制定 抗病毒药物 对策方面的作用。
证据基础与方法
这是一篇系统综述,总结了RNA病毒反向遗传学的进化、技术框架和优化策略。它使用代表所有主要 RNA 基因组 类型的三种原型病毒:SARS-CoV-2 (+ssRNA)、新城疫病毒 (NDV) 和甲型流感病毒。该综述详细阐述了感染性克隆构建、启动子系统、核酶和大型不稳定 基因组 的解决方案等核心模块。
共识与结论
共识是RNA病毒反向遗传学是疫苗开发、发病机制研究、病毒-宿主相互作用分析和高通量抗病毒药物筛选不可替代的工具。该评论强调了其在不同 RNA 病毒类型中的应用,并强调了其在基础病毒学、疫苗 创新和 抗病毒药物 设计中的重要性。
争议与缺口
目前的瓶颈包括救援效率低、cDNA遗传不稳定和生物安全隐患。审查将这些确定为推进该领域需要解决的关键挑战。此外,需要更标准化的模块化工具和生物安全工程来克服这些限制。
科研与实践启示
未来的进步应该整合CRISPR/Cas编辑、标准化模块化工具、生物安全工程、人工智能和大数据,以提高RNA病毒反向遗传学的效率和安全性。该审查还建议建立三层 抗病毒药物 筛选管道并开发非传染性替代平台以解决当前的限制。

Development, applications, and future prospects of RNA virus reverse genetics technology

Authors: Yu Guo, Ting Xue, Jianhua Wang, Xueqiao Xie, Furui Cao, Yijun Zhang, huiting Wang, 吕冰清
Original abstract (English)
Viral reverse genetics enables the rescue of infectious virions from cloned cDNA and serves as a core technique for mapping viral genotype–phenotype relationships, dissecting RNA viral life cycles, and developing antiviral countermeasures. This review systematically summarizes the evolution, technical framework and optimization strategies of RNA virus reverse genetics, with three prototype viruses covering all major RNA genome types: SARS-CoV-2 (+ssRNA), non-segmented negative-sense Newcastle disease virus (NDV), and segmented negative-sense influenza A virus. Core modules including infectious clone construction, diverse promoter systems, hammerhead/HDV ribozymes, and solutions for large unstable genomes such as BAC and ISA are elaborated. We summarize its irreplaceable applications in vaccine development, pathogenesis research, virus-host interaction analysis and high-throughput antiviral screening. Current bottlenecks include low rescue efficiency, cDNA genetic instability and biosafety hazards. We also introduce non-infectious surrogate platforms and establish a three-tier antiviral screening pipeline. Future advances will integrate CRISPR/Cas editing, standardized modular tools, biosafety engineering, AI and big data. Distinct from previous reviews focusing on single viral genera, this work conducts cross-type horizontal comparisons and summarizes universal technical obstacles and tailored optimizations, offering comprehensive references for basic virology, accelerated vaccine innovation and precise antiviral design.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on RNA virus reverse genetics technology, covering its development, applications, and future prospects. The central question involves understanding how this technology enables the rescue of infectious virions from cloned cDNA and its role in mapping viral genotype-phenotype relationships, dissecting RNA viral life cycles, and developing antiviral countermeasures.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic review that summarizes the evolution, technical framework, and optimization strategies of RNA virus reverse genetics. It uses three prototype viruses representing all major RNA genome types: SARS-CoV-2 (+ssRNA), Newcastle disease virus (NDV), and influenza A virus. The review elaborates on core modules such as infectious clone construction, promoter systems, ribozymes, and solutions for large unstable genomes.
Consensus and conclusions
The consensus is that RNA virus reverse genetics is an irreplaceable tool for vaccine development, pathogenesis research, virus-host interaction analysis, and high-throughput antiviral screening. The review highlights its applications across different RNA virus types and emphasizes its importance in basic virology, vaccine innovation, and antiviral design.
Controversies and gaps
Current bottlenecks include low rescue efficiency, cDNA genetic instability, and biosafety hazards. The review identifies these as key challenges that need to be addressed to advance the field. Additionally, there is a need for more standardized modular tools and biosafety engineering to overcome these limitations.
Research and practice implications
Future advances should integrate CRISPR/Cas editing, standardized modular tools, biosafety engineering, AI, and big data to improve the efficiency and safety of RNA virus reverse genetics. The review also suggests establishing a three-tier antiviral screening pipeline and developing non-infectious surrogate platforms to address current limitations.

A bivalent inactivated influenza vaccine incorporating epitope-optimized surface proteins confers cross-protective immunity against H9N2 influenza virus

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-07-30  |  Publisher: GOV.UK
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

全国流感和 COVID-19 监测 报告:2026 年 7 月 30 日(第 31 周) - GOV.UK

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 30 July 2026 (week 31) - GOV.UK
新闻简报The UK Health Security Agency's weekly surveillance report for 30 July 2026 (week 31) indicates low activity for influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in England. Influenza positivity rates remained at baseline levels (0.8% in labs, 5% in GP swabs), while COVID-19 PCR positivity in hospitals slightly increased to 1.1%. Hospital admissions for COVID-19 were low at 0.15 per 100,000. Other viruses like adenovirus and human metapneumovirus showed decreasing trends, with rhinovirus slightly increasing. The report uses experimental methods (MEM/MSD) for some indicators, and data remains provisional. No significant public health impact or elevated risk was identified.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 30 日。赛事数据涵盖第 30 周(2026 年 7 月 20 日至 26 日)。
地点与对象
一般人群,包括初级保健 (GP) 和医院环境。
事件
流感、COVID-19 和其他呼吸道病毒的 监测 每周报告。流感活动仍然较低且处于基线水平。COVID-19 活动也仍然较低,处于基线水平。资料来源:英国卫生安全局官方统计数据。
规模、影响与风险
Influenza positivity rate: 0.8% (laboratory surveillance), 5% (GP swabbing). COVID-19 PCR positivity in hospitals: 1.1%. Hospital admission rate for COVID-19: 0.15 per 100,000. No significant impact or elevated risk reported.
应对、状态与不确定性
监测 系统可运行。数据为临时数据,可能会进行回顾性更新。监测实验室采用移动流行病法(MEM)和均值标准差法(MSD),对部分指标进行实验。除了持续监测之外,没有提及具体的应对行动。
A high priorityPublic-health news

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 30 July 2026 (week 31) - GOV.UK

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency's weekly surveillance report for 30 July 2026 (week 31) indicates low activity for influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in England. Influenza positivity rates remained at baseline levels (0.8% in labs, 5% in GP swabs), while COVID-19 PCR positivity in hospitals slightly increased to 1.1%. Hospital admissions for COVID-19 were low at 0.15 per 100,000. Other viruses like adenovirus and human metapneumovirus showed decreasing trends, with rhinovirus slightly increasing. The report uses experimental methods (MEM/MSD) for some indicators, and data remains provisional. No significant public health impact or elevated risk was identified.

This publication is available at https://www.gov.uk/government/statistics/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-reports-2026-to-2027-season/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-report-30-july-2026-week-31 This report summarises the information from the surveillance systems which are used to monitor COVID-19 (caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2)), influenza, and diseases caused by seasonal respiratory viruses in England. The report is based on data up to week 30 of 2026 between 20 July and 26 July 2026. Main points The main messages of this report are: influenza activity remained low and circulating at baseline levels COVID-19 activity remained low and circulating at baseline levels respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) activity remained low and circulating at baseline levels Seasonal reports Annual reports for the 2025 to 2026 season: Influenza in the UK, annual epidemiological report: winter 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Epidemiology of COVID-19 in England (published 25 June 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in all GP patients: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in school-aged children: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in frontline healthcare workers: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Summary of all respiratory virus activity Influenza activity Influenza activity remained low and is circulating at baseline levels. Emergency department (ED) attendances for influenza-like-illness (ILI) remained low. Reporting of weekly influenza hospital admissions for the 2025 to 2026 season concluded in week 17. GP swabbing positivity Stable Baseline In week 29, among all tested samples, 0% were positive for RSV compared with 0% in the previous week Other viruses Indicator Trend Level [note 1] Comments Adenovirus Decreasing Baseline Adenovirus positivity (laboratory surveillance) decreased to 1.9% compared with 2.6% in the previous week Human metapneumovirus (hMPV) Decreasing Baseline hMPV positivity (laboratory surveillance) decreased to 0.1% compared with 0.3% in the previous week Parainfluenza Decreasing Baseline Parainfluenza positivity (laboratory surveillance) decreased to 1.5% compared with 1.9% in the previous week Rhinovirus Increasing slightly Baseline Rhinovirus positivity (laboratory surveillance) increased slightly to 9.5% compared with 9% in the previous week Note 1: these indicators use the moving epidemic method (MEM) and the mean standard deviation method (MSD) to define thresholds to determine their respective levels of activity. Further information on these methods can be found in Influenza surveillance in Europe: establishing epidemic thresholds by the Moving Epidemic Method and Setting thresholds to determine COVID-19 activity levels using the mean standard deviation (MSD) method, England, 2022 to 2024. The MEM approach is well-established for some influenza surveillance indicators, however, for other indicators both the MEM and MSD are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using MEM to using MSD. These approaches will be considered alongside expert opinion and triangulation of other data sources. Laboratory surveillance Laboratory-confirmed cases The Second Generation Surveillance System (SGSS) captures test result information for notifiable infectious diseases, including COVID-19 and influenza, from laboratories in England. The unified sample dataset (USD) stores all SARS-CoV-2 test results reported to SGSS, Respiratory DataMart and UKHSA laboratories, and is used to calculate the percentage of tests positive for SARS-CoV-2 among all SARS-CoV-2 tests conducted. SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) PCR positivity in hospital settings increased in week 30, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 1.1% up to Sunday 26 July 2026. This is compared with 0.9% on the previous Sunday. Influenza positivity in week 30 increased, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 0.8% up to Sunday 26 July 2026, compared with 0.7% on the previous Sunday. Due to an ongoing data quality issue resulting in more tests than usual being removed, historical positivity for influenza may be slightly lower in reports published from week 25 2025 onwards. Historical estimates will be updated once the affected data has been rectified. Figure 1. Rolling 7-day positivity of tests positive for SARS-CoV-2 among all reported SARS-CoV-2 tests, England 2022 to present [note 2] [note 3] Note 2: data from previous seasons is aligned by day. Note 3: testing policy and practice may change over time which can impact positivity rates, therefore comparisons over time should be interpreted with caution. Notable changes in testing policy occurred during 2022 to 2023, which are outlined in the data quality report. Figure 2. Rolling 7-day positivity of tests positive for influenza among all reported influenza tests, England 2022 to present [note 2] Note 2: data from previous seasons is aligned by day. Respiratory DataMart System Respiratory DataMart is a sentinel laboratory-based surveillance system where participating laboratories report positive and negative test results for a number of respiratory viruses from samples primarily taken in hospital. A small proportion of primary care samples are also included in this reporting. In week 30, data is based on reporting from 9 out of the 14 sentinel laboratories. In week 30, 2,561 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for influenza. There were 12 positive samples for influenza: 7 influenza A (not subtyped), 2 influenza A (H3N2), 0 influenza A (H1N1)pdm09, and 3 influenza B. Overall, influenza positivity decreased to 0.5% in week 30 compared with 0.8% in the previous week. In week 30, 3,224 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for SARS-CoV-2. There were 33 positive samples for SARS-CoV-2. SARS-CoV-2 positivity increased to 1% compared with 0.6% in the previous week, with the highest positivity in those aged between 15 and 44 years at 1.4%. RSV positivity remained stable at 0.3%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 1.5%. Adenovirus positivity decreased to 1.9%, with the highest positivity in those aged between 5 and 14 years at 12.3%. Human metapneumovirus (hMPV) positivity decreased to 0.1%, with the highest positivity in those aged 80 years and over at 0.5%. Parainfluenza positivity decreased to 1.5%, with the highest positivity in those aged between 15 and 44 years at 1.9%. Rhinovirus positivity increased slightly to 9.5%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 23.4%. DataMart data is provisional and subject to retrospective updates. Figure 3a. Respiratory DataMart weekly percentage of tests positive for influenza, SARS-CoV-2, RSV and rhinovirus, England [note 4] Note 4: shading represents 95% confidence intervals. Figure 3b. Respiratory DataMart weekly percentage of tests positive for adenovirus, hMPV and parainfluenza, England [note 4] Note 4: shading represents 95% confidence intervals. SARS-CoV-2 lineages UKHSA conducts genomic surveillance of SARS-CoV-2 lineages. This section provides an overview of circulating lineages in England, derived from data on sequenced PCR-positive SARS-CoV-2 samples in SGSS. The prevalence of UKHSA-designated lineages among sequenced cases is presented in Figure 4. To account for reporting delays, we report the proportion of lineages within COVID-19 cases that have had a sequenced positive sample between 8 June 2026 and 21 June 2026. Of those sequenced in this period: 25 % was classified as RV.1.3 25 % was classified as XFG.14.8 12.5 % was classified as RE.2 12.5 % was classified as RF.5 12.5 % was classified as RF.5.2 Note that low sequencing numbers, especially within the latest reporting period, will impact the accuracy of the prevalence estimates. These most recent figures should therefore be interpreted with caution. Note that lineages will be grouped independently from their parent lineage once they reach sufficient prevalence, and may be re-grouped into their parent lineage if their prevalence subsequently falls. The data quality report contains more information on lineage groupings. Figure 4. Prevalence of SARS-CoV-2 lineages amongst available sequenced cases for England from 21 July 2025 to 28 June 2026 Community surveillance Syndromic surveillance Syndromic surveillance collects data from various healthcare sources where presentations are classified by patterns of symptoms compatible with specific infections. In some settings, the syndromic diagnosis can be supplemented by (rapid) testing. In this report, ED attendances are displayed. Further details and data from other syndromic surveillance systems can be found in the syndromic surveillance weekly summaries. During the week ending on 26 July 2026, ED attendances for acute respiratory infections decreased and were similar to seasonally expected levels. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness remained low. ED attendance for influenza-like illness remained low and were similar to seasonally expected levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis (a syndrome related to RSV infection) remained low and were below seasonally expected levels. Daily NHS 111 acute respiratory infection triaged calls remained stable and were below seasonally expected levels. GP in-hours consultation rates for influenza-like illness were stable and similar to seasonally expected levels. GP out-of-hours daily contacts for acute respiratory infection remained stable and were below seasonally expected levels. Daily contacts for influenza-like illness remained stable and similar to seasonally expected levels. Figure 5. Daily emergency department attendances for acute respiratory infection nationally, England [note 5] Note 5: 7-day moving average is adjusted for bank holidays. Grey columns show weekends and bank holidays. Primary care surveillance Primary care surveillance is undertaken in collaboration with the Royal College of General Practitioners (RCGP) Research and Surveillance Centre (RSC), using a national sentinel surveillance system of around 2,000 GP practices covering over 20 million registered patients of all ages across England. More information on the methodology can be found in the data quality report. RCGP sentinel swabbing scheme in England There were fewer than 50 samples with a result for week 30. From week 40 2025, the RCGP sentinel swabbing scheme testing capability has been expanded to the UKHSA Bristol laboratory in addition to the UKHSA Colindale laboratory. Samples sent to Colindale are tested for influenza A and B, RSV A and B, SARS-CoV-2, hMPV, adenovirus, seasonal coronavirus and enterovirus/rhinovirus while samples sent to Bristol are tested for influenza A and B, RSV and SARS-CoV-2. 108 samples were taken in week 29 2026 through the GP sentinel swabbing, 100 were tested and 20 tested positive (Figure 6). As of week 4 2024, contemporaneous enterovirus differentiation has stopped. Starting from week 40 2025, samples with more than 7 days between the sample collection date and the symptom onset date have been excluded. In week 29 2026, influenza positivity was 5%, SARS-CoV-2 positivity was 3%, and RSV positivity was 0%. 44 samples were tested in Bristol and 56 samples were tested in Colindale. In Bristol, 29.5% of the samples tested were from the South West. In week 28 2026, influenza positivity was 0.9%, SARS-CoV-2 positivity was 2.6%, RSV positivity was 0%, adenovirus positivity was 1.3%, hMPV positivity was 1.2%, seasonal coronavirus positivity was 2.4%, and enterovirus/rhinovirus positivity was 7.6%. There were 33 samples tested in Bristol and 84 samples tested in Colindale. Due to the number of samples which have not yet been categorised, data should be interpreted with caution when compared with previous weeks. The weekly positivity is not calculated when the number of samples with a result is fewer than 50. Figure 6. Number of samples tested for respiratory viruses in England by week, GP sentinel swabbing scheme [note 6] Note 6: unknown category corresponds to samples with no result yet. Secondary care surveillance COVID-19 hospital admissions Surveillance of COVID-19 hospitalisations to all levels of care is mandatory, with data required from all acute NHS trusts in England. SARI Watch data is provisional and subject to retrospective updates. Rates are presented per 100,000 trust catchment population. Note that as of the week 29 2026 report, COVID-19 hospital admission rates per 100,000 trust catchment population from the 2024 to 2025 season onwards have been re-calculated using 2024 trust catchment populations. As a result of this methodological change, rates previously published for the 2024 to 2025 and 2025 to 2026 seasons may differ slightly from earlier versions due to updates in these population denominators. Weekly surveillance report for influenza, COVID-19, and other respiratory viruses. Influenza activity remained low and at baseline levels. COVID-19 activity also remained low and at baseline levels. Source: UK Health Security Agency official statistics.

Global youth competition on influenza and COVID-19 prevention and control - who.int
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2026 年 8 月 3 日至 9 月 13 日发起了流感和 COVID-19 预防和控制全球青年竞赛。该活动邀请学生和年轻专业人士制作简短视频,强调预防和控制这些疾病的措施的重要性。鼓励这些视频具有创新性,并解决社区中的错误信息或神话。该竞赛旨在提高认识并鼓励保护行为。世卫组织主题专家将使用指定的标准审查和评估视频。所提供的证据中没有报告确认的 暴发、病例、死亡或健康影响。该活动是一项有计划的举措,其对公众健康意识的潜在影响仍在调查中。

A high priorityPublic-health news

Global youth competition on influenza and COVID-19 prevention and control - who.int

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) launched a global youth competition on influenza and COVID-19 prevention and control from August 3 to September 13, 2026. The initiative invites students and young professionals to create brief videos that emphasize the importance of prevention and control measures against these diseases. The videos are encouraged to be innovative and address misinformation or myths in communities. The competition aims to raise awareness and encourage protective behaviors. WHO subject matter experts will review and assess the videos using a designated rubric. No confirmed outbreak, cases, deaths, or health impact is reported in the supplied evidence. The event is a planned initiative and remains under investigation for its potential impact on public health awareness.

Governing bodies World Health Assembly Executive Board Member States Portal Home/ Newsroom/ Events/ Detail/ Global youth competition on influenza and COVID-19 prevention and control Global youth competition on influenza and COVID-19 prevention and control 3 August – 13 September 2026 Reading time: 2 min (484 words) العربية Français Русский Español WHO invites students and young professionals to participate in this youth competition by developing brief videos that highlight the importance of influenza and/or COVID-19 prevention and control. The videos are encouraged to be innovative, address misinformation/myths, and encourage their community to protect themselves from seasonal influenza and COVID-19. Competition theme: “How do you shield your circle from respiratory infections, like influenza and COVID-19?” Eligibility: Participants must be students or young professionals between the ages of 18 and 35. Competition Prompts: The submission must include a brief video that responds to one or more of the following prompts: Prompt 1: Who needs protection from seasonal influenza/COVID-19? How can vulnerable groups protect themselves? Why is it important? Prompt 2: How do you respond to someone who has doubts about influenza and/or COVID-19 prevention and control in your community? Prompt 3: What myths about influenza and/or COVID-19 prevention and control are circulating in your community? How do you address them? Instructions for participants: Participants must address at least one of the competition prompts in their video. Students can work independently or in teams. Video can be a maximum of 90 seconds (1.5 minutes). Students can submit up to three videos. Submitted videos are encouraged to be creative. Video styles can include but are not limited to storytelling, interview, reel, animation, drawings, poem, dialogue, or other creative formats. If AI is used to generate any portion of the submission, it must be acknowledged in the submission form. The video may be in any language but must include English subtitles. Submissions will be assessed on the following criteria: Video message aligns with the competition theme and responds to at least one of the competition prompts Originality and creativity Accuracy and clarity of health information Visual style and storytelling quality The video includes English subtitles Responses to the submission form questions including the: Motivation/rationale for the video Prompts selected and how they were addressed Potential impact of the video Feasibility for dissemination in the community Confirmation that all filmed individuals consented to their appearance in the video and are over the age of 18 Confirmation that the individuals submitting the video are between the ages of 18-35 years Timeline: The competition and the submission form open on Monday 3 August 2026. All submissions must be received through the designated Microsoft Forms by Sunday 13 September 2026 at 23:45 CEST. Review process: WHO subject matter experts will review and assess the videos using a designated rubric. WHO will notify the winner(s) in October 2026. Winning videos: There will be one global winning video and up to six videos highlighted as runners-up. The winning videos will be featured by WHO on the competition webpage. The student(s) who submitted the winning video will: Be invited to present their video on a global influenza/COVID-19 prevention and control-focused webinar in October 2026 and Receive a certificate from WHO featuring their name(s) and the video title Related Video submission form (open 3 August–13 September): LINK Fact sheets Influenza (seasonal) 28 February 2025 Coronavirus disease (COVID-19) 27 November 2025

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

随着 Covid-19 和季节性流感病例激增,卫生部发布紧急咨询 - SGGP 英文版

Health Ministry issues urgent advisory as Covid-19, seasonal flu cases surge - SGGP English Edition
新闻简报由于 Covid-19 和 季节性流感 病例激增,越南卫生部于 2026 年 7 月 28 日发布紧急咨询。国家 监测 数据和包括国家热带病医院在内的主要医院的报告证实了咨询和入院人数的上升趋势。河内过去一周报告了 185 例病例,几乎是前一周的两倍,没有死亡记录。显性 NB.1.8.1 变体(Omicron 亚系)不会引起更严重的疾病。这一激增恰逢夏季旅行高峰、流动性增加以及炎热的天气迫使人们呆在室内。弱势群体,包括老年人和患有基础疾病的人,面临着较高的风险。该部正在加强 监测 的力度,并建议在高风险环境中使用口罩,强调这两种疾病都是地方性的,并且会自然波动。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月29日。事件日期:2026年7月28日发布公告。时间范围:近几周呈上升趋势。
地点与对象
地区:西太平洋。具体地点:河内(76个区/社区有185例病例)、国家热带病医院。受影响人群:普通公众,其中老年人、幼儿、孕妇和有基础疾病的人风险较高。
事件
卫生部于 2026 年 7 月 28 日发布紧急咨询,警告 Covid-19 和 季节性流感 病例激增。该事件是根据国家 监测 数据和医院报告确认的。尚未报告严重病例或死亡人数异常激增的情况。
规模、影响与风险
案例:Covid-19 咨询和入院呈上升趋势。河内过去一周报告病例185例,比前一周几乎翻倍,自年初以来累计病例588例。死亡人数:迄今为止没有死亡记录。风险:对于弱势群体来说风险较高。NB.1.8.1 变体(Omicron 亚系)占主导地位,但不会引起更严重的疾病。
应对、状态与不确定性
回应:卫生部正在与地方当局和医疗机构合作,监测趋势并部署有针对性的措施。监测 已加强。预防措施包括在医疗机构、公共交通工具和拥挤的场所佩戴口罩。不确定性:没有证据表明 SARS-CoV-2 或流感毒株的毒力发生异常变化。据描述,病例数自然波动。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health Ministry issues urgent advisory as Covid-19, seasonal flu cases surge - SGGP English Edition

News Brief
Vietnam's Ministry of Health issued an urgent advisory on July 28, 2026, due to a surge in Covid-19 and seasonal influenza cases. National surveillance data and reports from major hospitals, including the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, confirm a rising trend in consultations and admissions. Hanoi reported 185 cases over the past week, nearly doubling from the previous week, with no fatalities recorded. The dominant NB.1.8.1 variant (Omicron sub-lineage) is not causing more severe illness. The surge coincides with peak summer travel, increased mobility, and hot weather driving people indoors. Vulnerable populations, including the elderly and those with underlying conditions, face elevated risk. The Ministry is stepping up surveillance and advising mask use in high-risk settings, emphasizing that both diseases are endemic and naturally fluctuate.
Original source text (English)
August 3, 2026 at 20:21:38 SGGP Online SGGP Thể Thao SGGP Đầu tư Tài chính 中文版 SGGP Epaper SGGP English Edition Trang chủ National Ho Chi Minh City International Business Business Banking-finance Economy Self - introduction Stock market Highlights Vietnam's FDI inflows surge 58 percent to US$38 billion in seven months Agricultural, forestry, fishery exports rise 7.5 percent in first seven months Latest August stocks seen gaining, but caution advised August 3, 2026 at 09:09:00 Ho Chi Minh City launches retail stimulus to boost purchasing power August 3, 2026 at 09:14:04 Government targets MSCI stock market upgrade, fintech sandbox by 2030 August 3, 2026 at 03:37:52 Health Education Sports Culture/art Law Science/technology Lifestyle Travel Weather Health Health Ministry issues urgent advisory as Covid-19, seasonal flu cases surge SGGP July 29, 2026 at 07:09:10 The Ministry of Health issued a press release on July 28 outlining prevention measures against Covid-19 and seasonal influenza, warning of a heightened risk of respiratory disease transmission across the country. Doctors collect samples from Covid-19 patients. (Photo: SGGP) Data from the national infectious disease surveillance system and reports from major hospitals, including the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, indicate a rising trend in Covid-19 consultations and admissions in recent weeks. However, health officials emphasized that there has been no unusual spike in severe cases or fatalities related to the virus. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), the NB.1.8.1 variant, an Omicron sub-lineage, remained the dominant circulating strain globally and across the Western Pacific region through late June 2026. Current evidence suggests the variant neither causes more severe illness nor significantly diminishes the effectiveness of existing public health protocols. The spike coincides with Vietnam’s peak summer travel season. Increased domestic mobility, holiday gatherings, and crowded public events, combined with hot, humid weather that drives people indoors into poorly ventilated, air-conditioned spaces, have created fertile ground for the spread of airborne pathogens. Both Covid-19 and seasonal flu transmit primarily through respiratory droplets, with high-risk settings including mass transport, schools, medical facilities, and popular tourist destinations. Covid-19 can be particularly dangerous for people with underlying health conditions. (Photo: SGGP) The Ministry of Health noted that neither SARS-CoV-2 nor circulating influenza strains show evidence of abnormal changes in virulence. The vast majority of patients exhibit mild symptoms and recover with standard treatment. Nevertheless, the risk of severe complications remains elevated for vulnerable populations, including the elderly, young children, pregnant women, and individuals with compromised immune systems or underlying conditions such as cardiovascular disease, chronic lung disease, and diabetes. The Ministry said it is actively collaborating with local authorities, medical facilities, and specialized research institutes to closely monitor epidemiological trends, hospital admissions, severe cases, and circulating SARS-CoV-2 variants to continually assess risks and deploy targeted response measures. To proactively safeguard personal, family, and public health, the Ministry of Health advises the public to wear face masks properly when visiting medical facilities, using public transport, or spending time in crowded, enclosed, and poorly ventilated environments. The Ministry of Health has directed localities to step up surveillance of Covid-19 and seasonal influenza. Health officials emphasized that both Covid-19 and seasonal influenza are endemic infectious diseases whose case numbers naturally fluctuate in cycles and with changing weather conditions. While the public is urged to remain vigilant rather than complacent, officials stressed there is no cause for panic. Adopting simple, effective preventive measures and seeking medical evaluation at the onset of unusual symptoms will help protect individuals, their families, and the wider community. Meanwhile, local transmission is gathering speed in the capital. The Hanoi Center for Disease Control reported 185 Covid-19 cases across 76 wards and communes over the past week, nearly doubling the previous week's tally. This marks the third consecutive week of rising infections in Hanoi, bringing the city's total cases since the beginning of the year to 588. No fatalities have been recorded to date. Response, status and uncertainty
Response: The Ministry is collaborating with local authorities and medical facilities to monitor trends and deploy targeted measures. Surveillance has been stepped up. Prevention measures include wearing masks in medical facilities, public transport, and crowded spaces. Uncertainty: No evidence of abnormal changes in virulence in SARS-CoV-2 or influenza strains. Case numbers are described as naturally fluctuating.

The Ministry of Health advises on preventing COVID-19 and seasonal influenza. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The Ministry of Health in Vietnam has issued an advisory on preventing COVID-19 and seasonal influenza as cases trend upward in recent weeks. While no unusual increase in severe cases or deaths has been reported, the department notes heightened risk due to summer travel, gatherings, and enclosed spaces. High-risk groups, including the elderly, young children, pregnant women, and those with chronic conditions, are particularly vulnerable. The ministry recommends preventive measures such as mask-wearing in crowded areas, frequent handwashing, maintaining ventilation, and seeking medical advice when experiencing symptoms. Health authorities continue to monitor the situation closely.

VTV.vn - With the number of COVID-19 cases trending upwards in recent weeks, the Ministry of Health advises people to proactively take preventive measures, especially those at high risk. The Department of Disease Prevention ( Ministry of Health ) reported that the number of COVID-19 cases seeking examination and treatment at several medical facilities has tended to increase in recent weeks. However, to date, no unusual increase in severe cases or deaths has been recorded. You may also like According to the World Health Organization (WHO), by the end of June 2026, the NB.1.8.1 variant – a subtype of Omicron – was circulating primarily globally and in the Western Pacific region. There is currently no evidence to suggest that this variant causes more severe illness or reduces the effectiveness of existing disease control measures. The Ministry of Health notes that the summer vacation period, the tourist season, and the hot and humid weather lead to increased travel and large gatherings, while many activities take place in enclosed spaces with air conditioning. These factors increase the risk of transmitting respiratory diseases, especially COVID-19 and seasonal influenza. Although most cases of COVID-19 and seasonal influenza are mild and resolve after treatment, the disease can still become severe in the elderly, young children, pregnant women, people with cardiovascular disease, chronic lung disease, diabetes, kidney disease, immunocompromised individuals, or those undergoing cancer treatment. To proactively protect their health, the Ministry of Health recommends that people wear masks when visiting medical facilities, on public transport, in crowded places or enclosed, poorly ventilated spaces; wash their hands frequently with soap or disinfectant, cover their mouths when coughing or sneezing, and avoid touching their eyes, nose, and mouth. In addition, people need to keep their living and working spaces well-ventilated, maintain a balanced diet, exercise regularly, get enough sleep, and manage underlying health conditions effectively. When experiencing symptoms such as fever, cough, sore throat, runny nose, or fatigue, they should limit contact with others, proactively seek medical advice and examination when necessary, and avoid self-medication.
