B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

尽管有保护性抗体水平,儿童仍然更容易感染流感

Children remain more vulnerable to influenza despite protective antibody levels
新闻简报《自然通讯》最近发表的一项研究表明,尽管儿童具有保护性抗体水平,但仍然更容易感染流感。这项研究在南非三个流感季节(2016-2018)进行,分析了涉及 1,518 名参与者的 623 起感染事件。研究结果显示,即使考虑到季前免疫力,5 岁以下儿童面临的感染风险明显更高(比 40 岁以上成年人高 170-460%),并且病毒 RNA 排出时间要长 3-5 天。虽然 HAI 抗体滴度≥1:40 降低了四种研究病毒中的三种(甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、A(H3N2、B/维多利亚)的感染风险,但年龄仍然是一个强大的独立风险因素。该研究强调了传统 传播 模型的局限性,并强调需要更全面的方法来了解家庭中的流感动态。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究在 2016 年至 2018 年连续三个流感季节进行,每周测试两次。该研究结果已被《自然通讯》接受发表,但所提供的证据中没有报告确切的发表日期。
地点与对象
该研究是在南非使用 PHIRST 队列研究 进行的,包括农村和城市社区 327 个家庭的 1,518 名参与者。该分析包括 623 起涉及儿童和成人的不同感染事件。
事件
发表在《自然通讯》上的一项研究调查了季前免疫力、年龄、病毒脱落和社区暴露在塑造流感家庭 传播 动态中的作用。研究发现,尽管儿童具有保护性抗体水平,但仍然更容易感染流感,与成人相比,儿童的病毒脱落时间更长,感染风险更高。
规模、影响与风险
该研究分析了四种流感病毒的 623 次感染事件:甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 (n=133)、甲型H3N2流感病毒 (n=195)、B/Victoria (n=220) 和 B/Yamagata (n=75)。与 40 岁以上的成年人相比,5 岁以下儿童感染 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、B/Victoria 和 B/Yamagata 的风险高出 170% 至 460%。对于这四种病毒中的三种病毒,儿童释放病毒 RNA 的时间比老年人长 3 至 5 天。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用多重实时 rRT-PCR 来模拟病毒脱落轨迹,并使用贝叶斯分层分段线性模型来估计感染风险。局限性包括缺乏详细的家庭内接触者追踪、2-3 天的采样间隔以及使用 Ct 值作为病毒载量的间接指标。由于缺乏当前季节的 疫苗接种 数据和 COVID-19 之前的研究期,可能会限制对其他人群的适用性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Children remain more vulnerable to influenza despite protective antibody levels

News Brief
A recent study published in Nature Communications reveals that children remain more vulnerable to influenza despite having protective antibody levels. The research, conducted across three influenza seasons (2016-2018) in South Africa, analyzed 623 infection episodes involving 1,518 participants. Findings show that children under 5 face significantly higher infection risks (170-460% higher than adults over 40) and shed viral RNA 3-5 days longer, even when accounting for pre-season immunity. While HAI antibody titers ≥1:40 reduced infection risk for three of four studied viruses (A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2, B/Victoria), age remained a strong independent risk factor. The study highlights limitations in traditional transmission models and underscores the need for more comprehensive approaches to understand influenza dynamics in households.
Original source text (English)
Twice-weekly household testing across three influenza seasons shows why conventional antibody measures tell only part of the story about infection risk and prolonged viral shedding in children. Study: The roles of pre-season immunity, age, viral shedding, and community exposures in shaping influenza household transmission dynamics . Image Credit: Photoroyalty / Shutterstock In a recent study accepted for publication in the journal Nature Communications , researchers investigated the interplay between pre-season immunity, exposure intensity, viral shedding kinetics, and age in shaping the transmission dynamics of household influenza. The study’s analysis drew on the Prospective Household cohort study of Influenza, Respiratory Syncytial virus, and other respiratory pathogens community burden and Transmission dynamics in South Africa ( PHIRST ) cohort and comprised data from 1,518 participants across 327 households in rural and urban communities. The study’s findings revealed that pre-season serum hemagglutination inhibition ( HAI ) titers of at least 1:40 were associated with a reduced risk of infection for three of the four viruses studied. Furthermore, children exhibited age-dependent susceptibility and prolonged viral RNA shedding even after the models accounted for pre-season HAI antibody levels. Background Influenza has been recognized for decades as a major global respiratory health threat. However, because of the virus’s short shedding windows and high rates of asymptomatic infection, scientists have had difficulty characterizing key aspects of its real-world transmission dynamics. Previous research also indicates that traditional household transmission studies often overlook subclinical transmission events or rely on post-symptomatic clinical sampling. These limitations complicate efforts to develop comprehensive transmission models capable of disentangling how pre-existing humoral immunity, time-resolved viral shedding within households, and general community exposure are jointly associated with individual infection risk. About the Study The present study aimed to address these empirical uncertainties and inform future influenza prevention research by examining how measured immunity, age, viral shedding, and exposure were associated with the pathogen’s real-world transmission dynamics. The study dataset was obtained from PHIRST and included 1,518 participants across 327 households. PHIRST combined pre-season serum collection with twice-weekly virological testing of participants regardless of symptoms across three consecutive influenza seasons, 2016–2018. Viral shedding trajectories were subsequently modeled for 623 distinct infection episodes covering two influenza A subtypes and two influenza B lineages: A(H1N1)pdm09 (n = 133), A(H3N2) (n = 195), B/Victoria (n = 220), and B/Yamagata (n = 75). Multiplex real-time reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction ( rRT-PCR ) cycle threshold ( Ct ) values were used in Bayesian hierarchical piecewise-linear models, thereby enabling estimation of the rise-to-peak viral shedding and the subsequent clearance phase for each infection episode. These shedding parameters were subsequently integrated into daily multivariable transmission survival models to simultaneously quantify: 1. Household force of infection ( FOI ; the sum of infected household members’ estimated daily shedding intensities), 2. Community FOI (daily, site-specific cohort infection prevalence), 3. Pre-season HAI titers (≥1:40 versus <1:40), and 4. Individual demographic factors (age, sex, household size, and HIV status). Study Findings The study’s multivariable transmission survival models showed that exposure intensity was strongly associated with infection acquisition. Specifically, each 10-unit increase in the modeled household shedding-intensity measure was associated with a 65% to 95% higher infection hazard across subtypes and lineages, for example, A(H1N1)pdm09 hazard ratio ( HR ) 1.96, 95% confidence interval ( CI ): 1.58–2.44; B/Victoria HR 1.77, 95% CI : 1.42–2.20. Similarly, a one-percentage-point increase in site-specific cohort influenza prevalence, used as a proxy for community exposure, was associated with a 70% to 100% increase in infection risk across all four subtypes and lineages. In addition, participants with a pre-season HAI titer ≥1:40 had a significantly lower infection risk for three out of the four investigated circulating viruses. This elevated HAI titer was associated with a 70% reduction in acquisition risk for A(H1N1)pdm09 ( HR 0.30), 35% for A(H3N2) ( HR 0.65), and 52% for B/Victoria ( HR 0.48). However, the categorical threshold of 1:40 was not significantly associated with a lower risk for B/Yamagata, although higher titers were associated with lower risk when analyzed as a continuous variable. Age remained strongly associated with infection risk even after adjustment for exposure levels and baseline titers. Notably, children younger than 5 years had an estimated 170% to 460% higher risk of infection with A(H1N1)pdm09, B/Victoria, and B/Yamagata compared with adults older than 40 years. Children younger than 5 years were also estimated to shed viral RNA for 3 to 5 days longer than older adults for A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria after adjustment for pre-season HAI titer status. Conclusions The present study showed that pre-season serum HAI titers were associated with reduced influenza acquisition for three of the four viruses studied, while age remained associated with infection risk after adjustment for measured household and community exposures and HAI titers, and with shedding duration after adjustment for HAI titers and other individual factors. The study’s authors noted that the dataset was limited by the absence of detailed intra-household contact network tracking. The study also had 2- to 3-day sampling gaps and used Ct values as indirect proxies for viral load , which may have led to very short infections being missed and introduced uncertainty into viral-load estimates. Additional limitations included assuming that HAI titers remained constant throughout each season, excluding coinfections, using infections detected within the cohort as a proxy for community exposure, and having fewer B/Yamagata infections. The statistical analyses were not adjusted for multiple comparisons. The absence of current-season vaccination among participants and the use of pre- COVID-19 data may also limit the applicability of the findings to other populations and periods. The authors therefore suggested that further research should examine age-appropriate correlates of protection and immune mechanisms beyond serum HAI antibodies, including antibodies targeting neuraminidase and the HA stalk, as well as influenza-specific B- and T-cell memory. Journal reference: Sauter, M. K., et al. (2026). The roles of pre-season immunity, age, viral shedding, and community exposures in shaping influenza household transmission dynamics. Nature Communications . DOI : 10.1038/s41467-026-76037-x. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-76037-x
View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted across three consecutive influenza seasons from 2016 to 2018, with twice-weekly testing. The findings were accepted for publication in Nature Communications, but the exact publication date is not reported in the supplied evidence.
Location and population
The study was conducted in South Africa using the PHIRST cohort, comprising 1,518 participants across 327 households in rural and urban communities. The analysis included 623 distinct infection episodes involving children and adults.
Event
A research study published in Nature Communications investigated the roles of pre-season immunity, age, viral shedding, and community exposures in shaping influenza household transmission dynamics. The study found that children remain more vulnerable to influenza despite having protective antibody levels, with prolonged viral shedding and higher infection risk compared to adults.
Scale, impact and risk
The study analyzed 623 infection episodes across four influenza viruses: A(H1N1)pdm09 (n=133), A(H3N2) (n=195), B/Victoria (n=220), and B/Yamagata (n=75). Children under 5 years had a 170% to 460% higher risk of infection with A(H1N1)pdm09, B/Victoria, and B/Yamagata compared to adults over 40. Children shed viral RNA for 3 to 5 days longer than older adults for three of the four viruses.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used multiplex real-time rRT-PCR to model viral shedding trajectories and Bayesian hierarchical piecewise-linear models to estimate infection risk. Limitations include the absence of detailed intra-household contact tracking, 2-3 day sampling gaps, and the use of Ct values as indirect proxies for viral load. The absence of current-season vaccination data and pre-COVID-19 study period may limit applicability to other populations.

七岁男童出现严重甲型流感并发症,情况危急 - Dimsum Daily

Seven‐year‐old boy in critical condition with severe influenza A complications - Dimsum Daily
新闻简报香港一名七岁男孩因感染甲型流感 (H3) 出现严重并发症,目前情况危急。该孩子原本健康,于7月27日首次出现症状,并于8月1日迅速恶化,需要入住玛格丽特公主医院的儿科重症监护室。实验室检查证实甲型(H3)流感并发休克和脑病。尽管收到了2025/26 季节性流感 疫苗接种,但孩子的病情急剧恶化。香港的流感季节于 6 月下旬开始,目前正处于活跃期,10.65% 的呼吸道样本检测出季节性病毒呈阳性,其中主要是甲型流感 (H3),占 48%。卫生官员正在调查此案,并提醒公众保持警惕,因为预计未来几周流感活动将继续加剧。

时间
发表于2026-08-03;事件发生于2026年7月27日至8月3日期间。
地点与对象

一名七岁男童确诊严重感染甲型(H3)流感,并发休克及脑病,情况危急,入院儿科重症监护室。
规模、影响与风险
重症病例单一;香港的流感季节于六月下旬开始,10.65%的呼吸道样本对季节性流感病毒呈阳性(较前一周有所上升);甲型流感 (H3) 占已检测病例的 48%。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生防护中心正在进行调查;孩子已收到 2025/26 季节性流感 疫苗接种;一名家庭成员出现轻微呼吸道症状,并通过快速抗原检测检测出流感阳性;政府安排 2026/27 季节性流感 疫苗接种 计划。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seven‐year‐old boy in critical condition with severe influenza A complications - Dimsum Daily

News Brief
A seven-year-old boy in Hong Kong is in critical condition after developing severe complications from influenza A (H3) infection. The child, previously healthy, first showed symptoms on July 27 and deteriorated rapidly by August 1, requiring admission to Princess Margaret Hospital's paediatric intensive care unit. Laboratory tests confirmed influenza A (H3) complicated by shock and encephalopathy. Despite receiving the 2025/26 seasonal influenza vaccination, the child's condition worsened sharply. Hong Kong's influenza season, which began in late June, is currently active with 10.65% of respiratory specimens testing positive for seasonal viruses, primarily influenza A (H3) at 48%. Health officials are investigating the case and remind the public to remain vigilant as flu activity is expected to remain elevated in the coming weeks.

Home News Breaking news Seven‐year‐old boy in critical condition with severe influenza A complications Breaking news Local Seven‐year‐old boy in critical condition with severe influenza A complications By Erwin Wong - 8:31PM Mon August 3, 2026 176 Facebook Twitter WhatsApp Pinterest Telegram close Advertisements dimsumdailyhk [custom chain] curation-20260730-09:54 CANCEL NEXT VIDEO 3rd August 2026 – (Hong Kong) A seven‐year‐old boy is in critical condition after contracting a severe strain of influenza A, prompting health officials to remind the public to remain vigilant as seasonal flu activity intensifies. The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) announced today that it is investigating the serious paediatric case. The child, previously in good health, began experiencing a cough and runny nose on 27th July and developed a fever on 1st August. His condition worsened sharply on Monday, when he became less responsive and was taken to the Accident and Emergency Department at Princess Margaret Hospital. He was subsequently admitted to the paediatric intensive care unit. Laboratory tests confirmed infection with influenza A (H3). Doctors diagnosed influenza A complicated by shock and encephalopathy. The boy remains hospitalised in a critical state. Preliminary inquiries indicate that the child had received the 2025/26 seasonal influenza vaccination and had not travelled during the incubation period. One household member recently developed mild upper respiratory symptoms and tested positive for influenza using a rapid antigen test, but recovered without requiring hospital care. The CHP said investigations are ongoing. Hong Kong entered its influenza season in late June. According to the latest surveillance data, 10.65 per cent of respiratory specimens tested positive for seasonal influenza viruses last week, while the admission rate for influenza in public hospitals stood at 0.67 cases per 10,000 population. Both figures represent an increase compared with the previous week. Influenza A (H3) accounted for 48 per cent of detected cases, followed by influenza A (H1) at 41 per cent and influenza B at 11 per cent. Officials expect flu activity to remain elevated in the weeks ahead. Dr Edwin Tsui, Controller of the CHP, noted that the World Health Organisation has issued recommendations for the composition of the 2026‐2027 influenza vaccines for the northern hemisphere, with manufacturers now preparing supplies for deployment later this year. The Government is also making arrangements for the forthcoming 2026/27 Seasonal Influenza Vaccination Programme, with further details to be released in due course. Dr Tsui urged members of the public, particularly children, older people and individuals with underlying medical conditions or weakened immune systems, to seek prompt medical attention if they develop fever or respiratory symptoms, as deterioration can occur rapidly. Those living with vulnerable individuals should wear surgical masks at home if unwell and consult a doctor without delay. He also stressed the importance of maintaining household cleanliness, disinfecting frequently touched surfaces, practising proper hand hygiene and ensuring adequate indoor ventilation. Influenza A, Metapneumovirus, and Influenza B dominate respiratory virus circulation in Delhi NCR - The News Mill
新闻简报德里国家首都区正经历呼吸道病毒激增,其中以甲型流感 (H3N2) 为主,此外还有偏肺病毒、冠状病毒和乙型流感。医学专家将病毒数量上升归因于气温较低、湿度波动和空气污染,这些因素导致了室内拥挤。门诊中超过50%的儿童出现发烧、咳嗽、全身酸痛等症状,但大多数病例症状较轻且具有自限性。没有观察到住院人数显着增加。卫生专业人员建议采取预防措施,例如手部卫生、在拥挤的地方使用口罩以及避免与病人接触。建议家长监测儿童并就持续高烧或呼吸困难寻求医疗建议。这种情况反映了季风后和冬季过渡期间典型的季节性共循环模式。

应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Influenza A, Metapneumovirus, and Influenza B dominate respiratory virus circulation in Delhi NCR - The News Mill

News Brief
Delhi NCR is experiencing a surge in respiratory viruses, with Influenza A (H3N2) leading the circulation, alongside Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B. Medical experts attribute the rise to cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity, and air pollution, which promote indoor crowding. Over 50% of children in outpatient departments present with symptoms like fever, cough, and body aches, though most cases are mild and self-limiting. No significant hospitalization increase has been observed. Health professionals recommend preventive measures such as hand hygiene, mask use in crowded places, and avoiding contact with sick individuals. Parents are advised to monitor children and seek medical advice for persistent high fever or breathing difficulties. The situation reflects typical seasonal co-circulation patterns during post-monsoon and winter transitions.
Original source text (English)
Medical experts in Delhi NCR have reported a widespread circulation of respiratory viruses, with Influenza A (H3N2) identified as the primary cause of the current surge. This wave features severe symptoms, including high-grade fevers and recovery periods often extending beyond 10 days. Dr Arjun Dang, CEO of Dr Dang’s Lab, named Influenza A (H3N2), Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B as the main viral agents present in the region. He associated the surge with cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity, and increasing air pollution, conditions that encourage gatherings in crowded indoor spaces. - BJP chief Nitin Nabin pledges introspection after Bankipur and Datia by-polls, welcomes Gujarat win - Karnataka CM DK Shivakumar assures excluded Congress leaders will receive appropriate roles - Parliamentary panel chief urges Zuckerberg to apologise for removal of PM Modi’s video or face loss of safe harbour protection Dr Dhiren Gupta, a paediatric specialist at Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, noted that more than 50% of children in outpatient departments present symptoms such as fever, cough, and body aches. Speaking to ANI, Dr Arjun Dang said, “The respiratory pathogen pattern shows predominant circulation of viral agents, particularly Influenza A (H3N2), Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B, which aligns with the changing weather in Delhi NCR marked by cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity and rising air pollution leading to increased indoor crowding. The concurrent detection of multiple respiratory viruses suggests seasonal co-circulation/co-infections rather than isolated outbreaks, a pattern commonly observed during post-monsoon and winter transitions, while sporadic detection of Mycoplasma pneumoniae likely reflects background transmission or a secondary bacterial infection facilitated due to the immunocompromised state during viral infections.” Dr Dhiren Gupta, Paediatric Intensive Care Specialist, Pulmonologist and Allergist, said, “There has been a noticeable rise in influenza cases, including H3N2 virus infection, in India over the past month. In our outpatient department, more than 50% of children are currently presenting with symptoms of influenza such as fever, cough, cold, sore throat, and body ache.” He added, “The good news is that most of these cases are mild and self-limiting. At present, there is no significant increase in hospitalisation, and the majority of children are recovering well with routine care.” He advised parents to monitor their children and seek medical advice promptly if fever persists. “Parents are advised not to panic. Adequate hydration, good nutrition, and proper rest are important for recovery. Early consultation with a doctor is recommended if a child has a high fever, breathing difficulty, poor oral intake, or symptoms lasting more than a few days,” he said. Dr Dhiren also stressed the importance of hygiene and preventive measures to limit virus transmission: “Simple preventive measures such as regular hand washing, wearing masks in crowded places, and avoiding close contact with sick individuals can help reduce the spread of infection.”

Scale, impact and risk
Over 50% of children in outpatient departments present with influenza symptoms; cases described as mild and self-limiting with no significant increase in hospitalization; risk attributed to seasonal co-circulation and environmental factors.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health professionals advise monitoring, hydration, and early consultation for persistent symptoms; preventive measures include hand hygiene, mask use, and avoiding sick contacts; no major operational disruptions reported.
